---

## Root Cause: Three-Layer Path Mismatch

There are **three bugs compounding** to produce the "file not found" error. Here is each one explained precisely.

---

### Bug 1: `_local_generate_upload_url` uses a DIFFERENT UUID than `protocol.id`

**File:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/api-service/src/api_service/gcs.py`, lines 153-167

```python
def _local_generate_upload_url(filename: str) -> tuple[str, str]:
    blob_id = str(uuid4())          # <-- NEW UUID, unrelated to protocol.id
    blob_path = f"{blob_id}/{filename}"

    upload_dir = _LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR / blob_id
    upload_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

    upload_url = f"http://localhost:{api_port}/local-upload/{blob_path}"
    local_uri  = f"local://protocols/{blob_path}"  # stores blob_id UUID in the URI

    return (upload_url, local_uri)
```

This function creates a **fresh `uuid4()`** (`blob_id`) to name the upload subdirectory. This UUID is completely independent of the `Protocol.id` generated later. The `gcs_path` (`local://protocols/<blob_id>/<filename>`) returned here is what gets stored as `protocol.file_uri` in the database.

**In your specific case:**
- `blob_id` (directory UUID) = `83a87094-1c50-498a-a607-60524afe11d3`
- `protocol.id` = `71bc936b-a44f-4edd-b894-99f89866fa3c`

These are intentionally different — `file_uri` correctly references `83a87094-...` in the database. This by itself is not a bug, but it IS the source of confusion.

---

### Bug 2: `fetch_pdf_bytes` reconstructs the path using the wrong base directory

**File:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/tools/pdf_parser.py`, lines 75-86

```python
if file_uri.startswith("local://"):
    blob_path = file_uri[len("local://"):]          # strips "local://"
    # blob_path is now: "protocols/83a87094-1c50-498a-a607-60524afe11d3/Prot_000-a90aedda.pdf"

    upload_dir = os.environ.get("LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR", "uploads")
    local_path = Path(upload_dir) / blob_path       # joins LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR + blob_path
```

When `REDACTED`, the resulting path becomes:

```
REDACTED.pdf
```

But the **actual file on disk** is at:

```
REDACTED.pdf
```

The `protocols/` subdirectory is doubled up. Here is why:

---

### Bug 3: `_LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR` in `gcs.py` already includes `protocols/`, but `fetch_pdf_bytes` does NOT strip it from the URI

**In `gcs.py` (API service), line 34:**
```python
_LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR = Path(os.getenv("LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR", "uploads/protocols"))
```

When `LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR` env var is **not set**, this defaults to `uploads/protocols`. The file is physically saved at:
```
<LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR>/<blob_id>/<filename>
= uploads/protocols/83a87094-.../Prot_000-...pdf
```

The URI stored in `file_uri` is:
```
local://protocols/<blob_id>/<filename>
```

**In `pdf_parser.py` (processor service), line 77:**
```python
upload_dir = os.environ.get("LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR", "uploads")
```

The default fallback here is `"uploads"` (not `"uploads/protocols"`). Then it joins `LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR + blob_path` where `blob_path = "protocols/<blob_id>/<filename>"`.

**The mismatch depends on whether `LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR` is set:**

| Scenario | `LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR` set to | File stored at | `fetch_pdf_bytes` looks at | Match? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Dev (no env var) | not set | `uploads/protocols/<uuid>/<file>` | `uploads/protocols/<uuid>/<file>` | YES (accidentally works) |
| Dev (set to `uploads`) | `uploads` | `uploads/protocols/<uuid>/<file>` | `uploads/protocols/<uuid>/<file>` | YES |
| Your case | `/Users/.../uploads` | `/Users/.../uploads/protocols/<uuid>/<file>` | `/Users/.../uploads/protocols/<uuid>/<file>` | SHOULD WORK... |

Wait — let me re-examine the actual directory structure found on disk:

```
/uploads/
  83a87094-1c50-498a-a607-60524afe11d3/   ← blob_id dir, NO "protocols/" prefix
    Prot_000-a90aedda.pdf
  protocols/
    64c4831e-a417-4d7d-9917-c98f04be1641/  ← a different upload in protocols/
```

**This reveals the actual problem:** The file was uploaded to `/uploads/83a87094-.../` (no `protocols/` subdirectory), but `file_uri` stores `local://protocols/83a87094-.../filename`, so `fetch_pdf_bytes` looks for it at `REDACTED...` which doesn't exist.

The reason the file landed in `/uploads/83a87094-...` without the `protocols/` prefix is that **`LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR` was set to `/Users/.../uploads` at upload time**, so `_LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR` in `gcs.py` evaluated to `/Users/.../uploads` (the env var overrides the default `uploads/protocols`). The directory created was `/Users/.../uploads/<blob_id>/`, but the URI was hardcoded to `local://protocols/<blob_id>/<filename>` regardless.

---

### The Exact Bug in `_local_generate_upload_url`

```python
# gcs.py line 34
_LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR = Path(os.getenv("LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR", "uploads/protocols"))
#                                                        ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^
# When LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR=/Users/.../uploads (set in .env):
# _LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR = Path("/Users/.../uploads")  — NO "protocols" suffix

# gcs.py line 159
upload_dir = _LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR / blob_id
# = /Users/.../uploads/83a87094-.../   ← actual directory created on disk

# gcs.py line 164 — URI is HARDCODED to always include "protocols/"
local_uri = f"local://protocols/{blob_path}"
# = local://protocols/83a87094-.../Prot_000-a90aedda.pdf
```

When `LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR` is set to just `uploads` (or any path without `protocols` in it), the **file is saved without the `protocols/` prefix** in the path, but the URI is **always encoded with `local://protocols/`**. Then `fetch_pdf_bytes` joins `LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR + "protocols/" + blob_id + filename` = `uploads/protocols/83a87094-...` which doesn't match the actual location `uploads/83a87094-...`.

---

### Summary of the Call Chain

```
POST /protocols/upload
  → generate_upload_url(filename)
    → _local_generate_upload_url(filename)
      blob_id = uuid4()           # e.g. 83a87094-...  (NOT protocol.id = 71bc936b-...)
      upload_dir = _LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR / blob_id
                 = /Users/.../uploads/83a87094-...    # file physically saved here
      local_uri = f"local://protocols/{blob_id}/{filename}"
                                  # ^^^ "protocols/" hardcoded in URI

  → Protocol(file_uri="local://protocols/83a87094-.../Prot_000-...pdf") saved to DB

PUT /local-upload/83a87094-.../Prot_000-...pdf
  → local_save_file("83a87094-.../Prot_000-...pdf", bytes)
    → file_path = _LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR / blob_path
                = /Users/.../uploads/83a87094-.../Prot_000-...pdf  ✓ (file saved here)

ingest_node(state["file_uri"] = "local://protocols/83a87094-.../Prot_000-...pdf")
  → fetch_pdf_bytes("local://protocols/83a87094-.../Prot_000-...pdf")
    blob_path = "protocols/83a87094-.../Prot_000-...pdf"   # strips "local://"
    upload_dir = LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR = "/Users/.../uploads"
    local_path = /Users/.../uploads/protocols/83a87094-.../Prot_000-...pdf
                                    ^^^^^^^^^
                                    Extra "protocols/" — file NOT here!
    → FileNotFoundError
```

---

### The Fix

The inconsistency is in `gcs.py`. The URI constructor and the directory creator use different base paths when `LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR` is set without `protocols/`. There are two equivalent fixes:

**Option A — Make the URI match the actual save path** (remove `protocols/` from the hardcoded URI in `_local_generate_upload_url`):

```python
# gcs.py line 164 — change:
local_uri = f"local://protocols/{blob_path}"
# to:
local_uri = f"local://{blob_path}"
# Then fetch_pdf_bytes joins: LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR / "83a87094-.../<file>"  ✓
```

**Option B — Make the save path match the URI** (update `local_save_file` to strip the leading `protocols/` from the `blob_path` passed from the upload endpoint, or change what `local-upload` receives):

In `main.py`, the upload endpoint receives `blob_path` from the URL `/local-upload/{blob_path:path}` where `blob_path` is `83a87094-.../<filename>` (without `protocols/`). The save goes to `_LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR / blob_path`. But the URI stores `local://protocols/<blob_id>/...`. These are inconsistent by construction.

**Option A is the minimal, cleanest fix.** The `protocols/` in `local://protocols/...` is a semantic artifact that should be reflected only in what comes after the scheme — and `fetch_pdf_bytes` literally appends it to `LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR`, so the file directory must not also include `protocols/` separately.

    97→def _parse_gcs_uri(gcs_path: str) -> tuple[str, str]:
    98→    """Parse a gs:// URI into (bucket_name, blob_path).
    99→
   100→    Args:

   110→        raise ValueError(f"Invalid GCS URI: {gcs_path}. Must start with gs://")

   112→    parts = path.split("/", 1)
   113→    if len(parts) < 2 or not parts[1]:
   114→        raise ValueError(

   197→    Args:

   199→        data: Raw file bytes to store.
   200→    """
   201→    file_path = _LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR / blob_path
   202→    file_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   203→

   205→    hash_index_path = _LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR / ".hash-index.json"

   236→    try:

   238→    except OSError:
   239→        logger.warning("Could not write hash index to %s", hash_index_path)

   242→def local_get_file_path(blob_path: str) -> Path | None:
   243→    """Get the local filesystem path for a stored file."""
   244→    file_path = _LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR / blob_path
   245→    return file_path if file_path.exists() else None

   248→# --- GCS backend ---

   250→
   251→@gcs_breaker
   252→@_gcs_retry
   253→def _gcs_generate_upload_url(
   254→    filename: str,
   255→    content_type: str = "application/pdf",
   256→    expiration_minutes: int = 15,
   257→) -> tuple[str, str]:
   258→    """Generate a signed URL for uploading a file to GCS."""
   259→    blob_path = f"protocols/{uuid4()}/{filename}"
   260→
   261→    bucket_name = get_bucket_name()
   262→    client = get_gcs_client()
   263→    if client is None:
   264→        raise ValueError("GCS client initialization failed. Check GCP credentials.")
   265→
   266→    bucket = client.bucket(bucket_name)
   267→    blob = bucket.blob(blob_path)
   268→
   269→    signed_url = blob.generate_signed_url(
   270→        version="v4",
   271→        expiration=timedelta(minutes=expiration_minutes),
   272→        method="PUT",
   273→        content_type=content_type,
   274→    )
   275→
   276→    gcs_path = f"gs://{bucket_name}/{blob_path}"
   277→    return (signed_url, gcs_path)
   278→
   279→
   280→@gcs_breaker
   281→@_gcs_retry
   282→def _gcs_set_blob_metadata(gcs_path: str, metadata: dict[str, str]) -> None:
   283→    """Set custom metadata on a GCS blob."""
   284→    bucket_name, blob_path = _parse_gcs_uri(gcs_path)
   285→    client = get_gcs_client()
   286→    if client is None:
   287→        raise ValueError("GCS client unavailable. Cannot set metadata.")
   288→
   289→    bucket = client.bucket(bucket_name)
   290→    blob = bucket.blob(blob_path)
   291→    blob.metadata = metadata
   292→    blob.patch()
   293→    logger.info("Set metadata on %s: %s", gcs_path, metadata)
   294→
   295→
   296→@gcs_breaker
   297→@_gcs_retry
   298→def _gcs_generate_download_url(gcs_path: str, expiration_minutes: int = 60) -> str:
   299→    """Generate a signed URL for downloading a file from GCS."""
   300→    bucket_name, blob_path = _parse_gcs_uri(gcs_path)
   301→    client = get_gcs_client()
   302→    if client is None:
   303→        raise ValueError("GCS client unavailable. Check GCP credentials.")
   304→
   305→    bucket = client.bucket(bucket_name)
   306→    blob = bucket.blob(blob_path)
   307→
   308→    signed_url = blob.generate_signed_url(
   309→        version="v4",
   310→        expiration=timedelta(minutes=expiration_minutes),
   311→        method="GET",
   312→    )
   313→    return signed_url
   314→

     1→"""PDF fetcher tool: download PDF bytes from GCS or local storage.
     2→
     3→Pure function — no state dependency. Sends raw PDF bytes to Gemini
     4→via the File API (multimodal approach, no markdown conversion needed).
     5→
     6→Architecture note: This module is self-contained within protocol-processor-service
     7→and has NO dependency on api-service. Cross-service imports are reserved for
     8→workflow nodes that are integration glue.
     9→"""
    10→
    11→from __future__ import annotations
    12→
    13→import logging
    14→import os
    15→from pathlib import Path
    16→
    17→from shared.resilience import gcs_breaker
    18→from tenacity import (
    19→    before_sleep_log,
    20→    retry,
    21→    retry_if_exception_type,
    22→    stop_after_attempt,
    23→    wait_random_exponential,
    24→)
    25→
    26→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    27→
    28→
    29→@gcs_breaker
    30→@retry(
    31→    retry=retry_if_exception_type(Exception),
    32→    stop=stop_after_attempt(3),
    33→    wait=wait_random_exponential(multiplier=1, min=2, max=10),
    34→    before_sleep=before_sleep_log(logger, logging.WARNING),
    35→    reraise=True,
    36→)
    37→def _download_from_gcs(bucket_name: str, blob_name: str) -> bytes:
    38→    """Download PDF bytes from GCS with retry and circuit breaker.
    39→
    40→    Args:
    41→        bucket_name: GCS bucket name.
    42→        blob_name: Blob path within the bucket.
    43→
    44→    Returns:
    45→        Raw PDF bytes.
    46→    """
    47→    from google.cloud import storage  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
    48→
    49→    client = storage.Client()
    50→    bucket = client.bucket(bucket_name)
    51→    blob = bucket.blob(blob_name)
    52→    return blob.download_as_bytes()
    53→
    54→
    55→async def fetch_pdf_bytes(file_uri: str) -> bytes:
    56→    """Fetch PDF bytes from a file URI.
    57→
    58→    Supports two URI schemes:
    59→    - local:// : Reads from a local upload directory (for dev/test).
    60→      The path after 'local://' is the relative blob path within
    61→      LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR (env var, default 'uploads'). Must match the API
    62→      service's LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR so both resolve to the same protocols tree.
    63→    - gs:// : Downloads from Google Cloud Storage.
    64→
    65→    Args:
    66→        file_uri: URI of the PDF file (local:// or gs://).
    67→
    68→    Returns:
    69→        Raw PDF bytes.
    70→
    71→    Raises:
    72→        FileNotFoundError: If local file does not exist.
    73→        ValueError: If URI scheme is not recognized.
    74→    """
    75→    if file_uri.startswith("local://"):
    76→        blob_path = file_uri[len("local://") :]
    77→        upload_dir = os.environ.get("LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR", "uploads")
    78→        local_path = Path(upload_dir) / blob_path
    79→        if not local_path.exists():
    80→            raise FileNotFoundError(
    81→                f"Local PDF not found at {local_path}. "
    82→                f"Ensure LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR is set correctly "
    83→                f"(current: {upload_dir}) and the file has been uploaded."
    84→            )
    85→        logger.info("Reading PDF from local path: %s", local_path)
    86→        return local_path.read_bytes()
    87→
    88→    if file_uri.startswith("gs://"):
    89→        path_without_scheme = file_uri[len("gs://") :]
    90→        bucket_name, _, blob_name = path_without_scheme.partition("/")
    91→        logger.info(
    92→            "Downloading PDF from GCS: bucket=%s, blob=%s",
    93→            bucket_name,
    94→            blob_name,
    95→        )
    96→        return _download_from_gcs(bucket_name, blob_name)
    97→
    98→    raise ValueError(
    99→        f"Unknown file URI scheme: {file_uri}. Expected 'local://' or 'gs://' prefix."
   100→    )
   101→
```

Now I can see the exact mismatch. Here's what happens:

1. **`gcs.py:34`**: `_LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR = Path(os.getenv("LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR", "uploads/protocols"))` — when `LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR` is set to `/Users/.../uploads`, the `protocols/` suffix is lost
2. **`gcs.py:159`**: File saved to `_LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR / blob_id` = `/uploads/<blob_id>/...`
3. **`gcs.py:164`**: URI stored as `local://protocols/<blob_id>/...` — hardcoded `protocols/`
4. **`pdf_parser.py:76-78`**: Strips `local://` → `protocols/<blob_id>/...`, joins with `LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR` → `/uploads/protocols/<blob_id>/...` — **path doesn't exist**

The fix: remove the hardcoded `protocols/` from the URI in `gcs.py`, since the file is saved directly under `_LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR` (which may or may not include `protocols/`). The URI should just reflect the blob path relative to `_LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR`.

However, the Makefile always sets it to `$(pwd)/uploads`, so in practice both services use the same explicit value. But the defaults should still be consistent. Since both the Makefile and docker-compose set it to `uploads` (without `protocols/`), the `gcs.py` default should match:

> AGENT

Good — the files are at `/uploads/<blob_id>/` (no `protocols/` prefix), confirming the fix is correct. There's also an old `protocols/` directory from previous behavior.

Here's a summary of the three fixes:

1. **`gcs.py:34`** — Changed default `_LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR` from `"uploads/protocols"` to `"uploads"` to match `pdf_parser.py` and the Makefile/docker-compose settings.

2. **`gcs.py:164`** — Changed URI from `local://protocols/{blob_path}` to `local://{blob_path}`. The `protocols/` prefix was hardcoded in the URI but not in the actual save path, causing `fetch_pdf_bytes` to look for the file in a non-existent `protocols/` subdirectory.

3. **`gcs.py:173`** — Updated `_local_generate_download_url` to strip `local://` instead of `local://protocols/` to match the new URI format.

The root cause: when `LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR` was set explicitly (as the Makefile does), `_LOCAL_UPLOAD_DIR` lost the `protocols/` suffix, so files were saved at `<uploads>/<blob_id>/<file>`. But the URI still hardcoded `local://protocols/<blob_id>/<file>`, making `fetch_pdf_bytes` look at `<uploads>/protocols/<blob_id>/<file>` — which doesn't exist.

< def _cleanup_orphan_traces() -> None:
<     """Close stale IN_PROGRESS MLflow traces from previous crashed runs.
< 
<     Runs once at module import (service startup). Idempotent -- safe to
<     call multiple times. Only closes traces older than 1 hour to avoid
<     closing currently-running pipelines.
<     """
<     import time as _time
< 
<     tracking_uri = os.getenv("MLFLOW_TRACKING_URI")
<     if not tracking_uri:
<         return
<     try:
<         import mlflow
< 
<         client = mlflow.MlflowClient()
<         cutoff_ms = int((_time.time() - 3600) * 1000)  # 1 hour ago
< 
<         # MLflow 3.x: search for traces with IN_PROGRESS status
<         try:
<             traces = client.search_traces(
<                 experiment_ids=["0"],  # Default experiment
<                 filter_string="status = 'IN_PROGRESS'",
<                 max_results=50,
<             )
<         except (AttributeError, TypeError):
<             # search_traces may not exist or have different signature
<             logger.debug("MLflow search_traces API not available for orphan cleanup")
<             return
< 
<         closed = 0
<         for trace in traces:
<             trace_ts = getattr(trace.info, "timestamp_ms", None) or 0
<             if trace_ts < cutoff_ms:
<                 try:
<                     client.end_trace(
<                         request_id=trace.info.request_id,
<                         status="ERROR",
<                     )
<                     closed += 1
<                 except Exception:
<                     pass  # Trace may have already been closed
<         if closed:
<             logger.info(
<                 "Startup orphan cleanup: closed %d stale IN_PROGRESS trace(s)",
<                 closed,
<             )
<     except Exception:
<         logger.debug("Orphan trace cleanup skipped (non-fatal)", exc_info=True)
< 
< 
< # Run once at startup to clean up orphaned traces from previous crashes
< _cleanup_orphan_traces()

185,200d129
< # KNOWN LIMITATION: MLflow ContextVar async boundary (Issue #8)
< #
< # MLflow's trace context uses Python ContextVars which do NOT propagate
< # across asyncio task boundaries created by asyncio.create_task() or
< # asyncio.gather(). This means:
< #   - The parent trace in trigger.py correctly wraps the full pipeline run
< #   - Child spans created inside LangGraph nodes are attached to the parent
< #     trace because LangGraph uses `await` (not create_task) for node calls
< #   - However, if future code uses asyncio.gather() for parallel node
< #     execution, child spans may detach from the parent trace
< #   - Workaround: Pass trace context explicitly via PipelineState if parallel
< #     node execution is added
< #
< # This is a known MLflow limitation, not a bug in our code.
< 
<

< async def _ground_entity_parallel(
<     entity: dict[str, Any],
<     router: TerminologyRouter,
<     criterion_text: str,
<     entity_num: int,
<     total: int,
<     semaphore: asyncio.Semaphore,
< ) -> tuple[EntityGroundingResult | None, str | None]:
<     """Ground a single entity with concurrency control and timing.
< 
<     Acquires the semaphore before grounding to cap concurrent API calls.
<     Returns a (result, error) tuple for error isolation -- one entity failure
<     does not crash others in the asyncio.gather batch.
< 
<     Args:
<         entity: Entity dict with text, entity_type, criterion_id.
<         router: TerminologyRouter singleton for API dispatch.
<         criterion_text: Full criterion text for context.
<         entity_num: 1-based index for logging.
<         total: Total entity count for logging.
<         semaphore: Concurrency limiter (Semaphore(4)).
275,314d249
<     Returns:
<         (EntityGroundingResult, None) on success, or (None, error_message) on failure.
<     """
<     async with semaphore:
<         entity_text = entity.get("text", "")[:50]
<         entity_type = entity.get("entity_type", "")
<         start = time.monotonic()
<         logger.info(
<             "Grounding entity %d/%d: '%s' (type=%s) — start",
<             entity_num,
<             total,
<             entity_text,
<             entity_type,
<         )
<         try:
<             result = await _ground_entity_with_retry(entity, router, criterion_text)
<             elapsed = time.monotonic() - start
<             logger.info(
<                 "Entity %d/%d '%s' grounded in %.1fs: code=%s, conf=%.2f",
<                 entity_num,
<                 total,
<                 entity_text,
<                 elapsed,
<                 result.selected_code or "N/A",
<                 result.confidence,
<             )
<             return (result, None)
<         except Exception as e:
<             elapsed = time.monotonic() - start
<             logger.error(
<                 "Entity %d/%d '%s' failed in %.1fs: %s",
<                 entity_num,
<                 total,
<                 entity_text,
<                 elapsed,
<                 e,
<             )
<             return (None, str(e))

< def _list_protocols_deduplicated(
<     page: int,
<     page_size: int,
<     status: str | None,
<     db: Session,
< ) -> ProtocolListResponse:
<     """Return one protocol per unique title (latest version) with version_count.
< 
<     Excludes archived protocols. When status filter is provided, only titles
<     whose latest version matches that status are returned.
<     """
<     # Subquery: latest created_at per title (excluding archived)
<     latest_subq = (
<         select(Protocol.title, func.max(Protocol.created_at).label("max_created"))
<         .where(Protocol.status != "archived")
<         .group_by(Protocol.title)
<         .subquery()
<     )
< 
<     # Count subquery: number of (non-archived) versions per title
<     count_subq = (
<         select(Protocol.title, func.count(col(Protocol.id)).label("version_count"))
<         .where(Protocol.status != "archived")
<         .group_by(Protocol.title)
<         .subquery()
<     )
< 
<     # Join Protocol to latest_subq to get the canonical (newest) row per title
<     dedup_stmt = select(Protocol).join(
<         latest_subq,
<         (col(Protocol.title) == latest_subq.c.title)
<         & (col(Protocol.created_at) == latest_subq.c.max_created),
<     )
< 
<     if status:
<         dedup_stmt = dedup_stmt.where(Protocol.status == status)
< 
<     # Count total unique titles (for pagination)
<     count_stmt = select(func.count()).select_from(dedup_stmt.subquery())
<     total = db.exec(count_stmt).one()
< 
<     # Apply pagination and ordering
<     dedup_stmt = (
<         dedup_stmt.order_by(Protocol.created_at.desc())  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
<         .offset((page - 1) * page_size)
<         .limit(page_size)
<     )
< 
<     protocols = db.exec(dedup_stmt).all()
<     pages = ceil(total / page_size) if total > 0 else 1
< 
<     # Build version_count lookup from count subquery
<     version_counts: dict[str, int] = {
<         row[0]: row[1]
<         for row in db.exec(select(count_subq.c.title, count_subq.c.version_count)).all()
<     }
< 
<     return ProtocolListResponse(
<         items=[
<             _protocol_to_response(p, version_count=version_counts.get(p.title, 1))
<             for p in protocols
<         ],
<         total=total,
<         page=page,
<         page_size=page_size,
<         pages=pages,
<     )

Now I have the full picture. Here's a comprehensive summary of everything that was in the backup but is **missing from the current repo**:

---

## Changes Lost from Backup → Current

### 1. **`graph.py` — AsyncPostgresSaver checkpointer** (REGRESSION)
The backup had upgraded from sync `PostgresSaver` to `AsyncPostgresSaver`:
- `_get_checkpointer()` → `_get_checkpointer_async()` (async, uses `AsyncPostgresSaver.from_conn_string`)
- `get_graph()` → `async get_graph()` 
- Proper async context manager lifecycle (`__aenter__` / `asetup()`)

Current repo still has the old sync version.

### 2. **`trigger.py` — Major improvements lost**
- **Orphan trace cleanup at startup** (`_cleanup_orphan_traces()`) — closes stale IN_PROGRESS MLflow traces from crashed runs
- **MLflow ContextVar documentation comment** (Issue #8)
- **`mlflow.langchain.autolog(run_tracer_inline=True)`** — the `run_tracer_inline` flag is gone
- **Graph passed as parameter** to `_run_pipeline()` instead of lazy-imported inside it — backup had `graph` created once in the sync handler, passed into the async pipeline
- **`succeeded` flag** for smarter trace cleanup — only force-closes as ERROR on failure, lets context manager handle success
- **Status set to `extraction_failed`** → backup uses `pipeline_failed`

### 3. **`ground.py` — Parallel grounding with asyncio.gather** (MAJOR)
The backup had a significant performance improvement:
- `_ground_entity_parallel()` helper with `asyncio.Semaphore(4)` for concurrency control
- `asyncio.gather(*tasks)` for parallel entity grounding instead of sequential `for` loop
- Consent entities pre-filtered before the gather batch
- `PIPELINE_MAX_ENTITIES` env var for dev/debug truncation
- Detailed per-entity timing logs

Current repo does sequential grounding in a `for` loop.

### 4. **`parse.py` — Dev knob lost**
- `PIPELINE_MAX_CRITERIA` env var for truncating criteria during dev/debug runs

### 5. **`persist.py` — Audit log + safety improvements**
- `AuditLog` entry emitted on `protocol_status_change` during persist
- Guard: `if not isinstance(existing, dict): existing = {"original_conditions": existing}`

### 6. **`protocols.py` — Several features lost**
- **`confirm-upload` flow**: Event deferred to `confirm-upload` endpoint so the file exists when the processor runs. Current fires the event immediately on POST (before file is uploaded!)
- **`archive_protocol` endpoint**: POST `/{protocol_id}/archive` for dead-letter/failed protocols
- **Deduplication**: `?deduplicate=True` query param returning one protocol per unique title with `version_count`
- **`error_reason` field** on `ProtocolResponse`
- **`version_count` field** on `ProtocolResponse`
- **`col()` wrapper** for proper SQLAlchemy typing (backup uses `col(Criteria.id)`, current has `Criteria.id` with type-ignore)

### 7. **`gcs.py` — Upload URI + path consistency**
The backup had the correct fix with `local://` URI scheme (no hardcoded `protocols/`), plus backward-compatible stripping of both `local://protocols/` and `local://`. Current had the bug we already fixed above.

### 8. **`pdf_parser.py` — Default path alignment**
Backup defaults to `uploads/protocols` to match the api-service. Current defaults to `uploads`.

### 9. **`outbox.py` — ContextVar propagation fix**
- Dedicated `ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=2)` to avoid starving FastAPI's executor
- `contextvars.copy_context()` to propagate MLflow ContextVars across thread boundaries — this fixes the "created in a different Context" WARNING you're seeing

### 10. **`auth.py` — OAuth guard**
- Blocks redirect when OAuth is unconfigured or uses a known-deleted client ID (prevents IDE popup loops)

### 11. **`main.py` — `run_tracer_inline=True`**
MLflow autolog flag lost.

### 12. **`routing.yaml` + `system.jinja2` — Config improvements**
- `Other: []` catch-all entity type in routing config
- Comments about RxNorm drug class behavior
- Prompt instruction to NOT extract consent/participation statements

### 13. **`entity_decomposer.py` — Type safety**
- Proper `cast(DecomposedEntityList, result)` instead of `# type: ignore`

### 14. **Entire `umls-mcp-server` service** — missing from current repo

### 15. **`test_sentence_extraction.py`** — test file missing from current

### 16. **Frontend: `ActivityFeed.tsx`** — missing component

---

**The most critical regressions are:**
1. The **confirm-upload flow** (#6) — current fires the processor event before the file is uploaded, directly causing the FileNotFoundError
2. The **parallel grounding** (#3) — significant performance loss
3. The **outbox ContextVar fix** (#9) — causes the MLflow warnings you're seeing
4. The **async checkpointer** (#1) — sync PostgresSaver in an async pipeline

Want me to restore all of these from the backup?

     1→"""FastAPI router for protocol upload, confirmation, listing, and detail.
     2→
     3→Provides endpoints for:
     4→- POST /protocols/upload: Generate signed URL for direct browser-to-GCS upload
     5→- POST /protocols/{id}/confirm-upload: Confirm and quality-check
     6→- GET /protocols: Paginated protocol list with optional status filter
     7→- GET /protocols/{protocol_id}: Protocol detail view with quality metadata
     8→- POST /protocols/{protocol_id}/retry: Resume failed protocol from LangGraph checkpoint
     9→- GET /protocols/{protocol_id}/batches: All batches including archived (timeline)
    10→"""
    11→
    12→from __future__ import annotations
    13→
    14→import base64
    15→import logging
    16→from datetime import datetime, timedelta, timezone
    17→from math import ceil
    18→from typing import Any

    20→from events_py.models import DomainEventKind
    21→from events_py.outbox import persist_with_outbox

    24→from shared.models import Criteria, CriteriaBatch, Protocol, Review
    25→from shared.resilience import gemini_breaker
    26→from sqlmodel import Session, col, func, select

    29→from api_service.fuzzy_matching import inherit_reviews_for_batch

    32→    set_blob_metadata,
    33→)
    34→from api_service.quality import (
    35→    compute_quality_score,
    36→    quality_result_to_metadata,
    37→)
    38→
    39→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    40→
    41→router = APIRouter(prefix="/protocols", tags=["protocols"])
    42→
    43→
    44→# --- Request/Response models ---
    45→
    46→
    47→class UploadRequest(BaseModel):
    48→    """Request body for initiating a protocol PDF upload."""
    49→
    50→    filename: str
    51→    content_type: str = "application/pdf"

    54→
    55→class UploadResponse(BaseModel):

    58→    protocol_id: str

    64→class ConfirmUploadRequest(BaseModel):
    65→    """Request body for confirming upload and triggering quality analysis.
    66→
    67→    The pdf_bytes_base64 field is optional. If provided, the server
    68→    decodes it and runs quality analysis. If omitted, quality scoring
    69→    is skipped (useful when the browser uploaded directly to GCS).
    70→    """
    71→
    72→    pdf_bytes_base64: str | None = None
    73→
    74→
    75→class ProtocolResponse(BaseModel):
    76→    """Response model for a single protocol."""
    77→
    78→    id: str
    79→    title: str
    80→    file_uri: str
    81→    status: str

    83→    quality_score: float | None

    86→    updated_at: datetime
    87→    error_reason: str | None = None

    89→
    90→

    94→    items: list[ProtocolResponse]

    99→
   100→
   101→class RetryResponse(BaseModel):
   102→    """Response for retry endpoint."""

   106→
   107→
   108→class ReExtractResponse(BaseModel):
   109→    """Response for re-extraction endpoint."""
   110→
   111→    status: str
   112→    protocol_id: str
   113→    archived_batches: int
   114→
   115→
   116→class BatchSummaryResponse(BaseModel):
   117→    """Response model for a single batch summary in the protocol timeline.
   118→
   119→    Includes ALL batches (archived and non-archived) ordered chronologically.
   120→    Used by the batch timeline view to show re-extraction history.
   121→    """
   122→
   123→    id: str
   124→    protocol_id: str
   125→    status: str
   126→    is_archived: bool
   127→    criteria_count: int
   128→    reviewed_count: int
   129→    extraction_model: str | None
   130→    created_at: datetime
   131→
   132→
   133→# --- Endpoints ---
   134→
   135→
   136→@router.post("/upload", response_model=UploadResponse)
   137→def upload_protocol(
   138→    body: UploadRequest,
   139→    db: Session = Depends(get_db),
   140→) -> UploadResponse:
   141→    """Generate a signed URL for uploading a protocol PDF.
   142→
   143→    Validates the content type (must be PDF) and file size (max 50MB),
   144→    creates a Protocol record in the database, and returns a signed
   145→    URL for direct browser upload to GCS.
   146→    """
   147→    # Validate content type
   148→    if body.content_type != "application/pdf":
   149→        raise HTTPException(
   150→            status_code=400,
   151→            detail="Only PDF files are accepted",
   152→        )
   153→
   154→    # Validate file size (50MB limit)
   155→    max_size = 50 * 1024 * 1024
   156→    if body.file_size_bytes > max_size:
   157→        raise HTTPException(
   158→            status_code=400,
   159→            detail="File size exceeds 50MB limit",
   160→        )
   161→
   162→    # Generate signed upload URL
   163→    signed_url, gcs_path = generate_upload_url(
   164→        filename=body.filename,
   165→        content_type=body.content_type,
   166→    )
   167→
   168→    # Create protocol record with title from filename (strip .pdf)
   169→    title = body.filename
   170→    if title.lower().endswith(".pdf"):
   171→        title = title[:-4]
   172→
   173→    protocol = Protocol(
   174→        title=title,
   175→        file_uri=gcs_path,
   176→        status="uploaded",
   177→    )
   178→
   179→    # Persist protocol record only — the ProtocolUploaded event is deferred
   180→    # to confirm-upload so the file actually exists when the processor runs.
   181→    db.add(protocol)
   182→    db.commit()
   183→
   184→    # Check circuit breaker states — if critical services are down,
   185→    # mark protocol as pending with warning (per locked decision)
   186→    processing_warning: str | None = None
   187→    if gemini_breaker.current_state != "closed":
   188→        protocol.status = "pending"
   189→        processing_warning = "Processing delayed — AI service temporarily unavailable"
   190→        protocol.metadata_ = {
   191→            **protocol.metadata_,
   192→            "processing_warning": processing_warning,
   193→        }
   194→        db.add(protocol)
   195→        db.commit()
   196→
   197→    return UploadResponse(
   198→        protocol_id=protocol.id,
   199→        upload_url=signed_url,
   200→        gcs_path=gcs_path,
   201→        warning=processing_warning,
   202→    )
   203→
   204→
   205→@router.post(
   206→    "/{protocol_id}/confirm-upload",
   207→    response_model=ProtocolResponse,
   208→)
   209→def confirm_upload(
   210→    protocol_id: str,
   211→    body: ConfirmUploadRequest,
   212→    db: Session = Depends(get_db),
   213→) -> ProtocolResponse:
   214→    """Confirm a protocol upload and optionally trigger quality analysis.
   215→
   216→    Looks up the protocol by ID. If pdf_bytes_base64 is provided,
   217→    decodes the PDF bytes and computes a quality score including
   218→    text extractability, page count, and encoding type.
   219→    """
   220→    protocol = db.get(Protocol, protocol_id)
   221→    if not protocol:
   222→        raise HTTPException(
   223→            status_code=404,
   224→            detail=f"Protocol {protocol_id} not found",
   225→        )
   226→
   227→    if body.pdf_bytes_base64:
   228→        try:
   229→            pdf_bytes = base64.b64decode(body.pdf_bytes_base64)
   230→            result = compute_quality_score(pdf_bytes)
   231→
   232→            protocol.quality_score = result.overall_score
   233→            protocol.page_count = result.page_count
   234→            protocol.metadata_ = {"quality": result.model_dump()}
   235→
   236→            # Store quality metadata on GCS blob (best-effort)
   237→            try:
   238→                set_blob_metadata(
   239→                    protocol.file_uri,
   240→                    quality_result_to_metadata(result),
   241→                )
   242→            except Exception:
   243→                logger.warning(
   244→                    "Failed to set GCS metadata for %s",
   245→                    protocol.file_uri,
   246→                    exc_info=True,
   247→                )
   248→        except Exception:
   249→            logger.warning(
   250→                "Failed to compute quality score for protocol %s",
   251→                protocol_id,
   252→                exc_info=True,
   253→            )
   254→
   255→    # Now that the file is confirmed uploaded, publish the outbox event
   256→    # to trigger the protocol processor pipeline.
   257→    persist_with_outbox(
   258→        session=db,
   259→        entity=protocol,
   260→        event_type=DomainEventKind.PROTOCOL_UPLOADED,
   261→        aggregate_type="protocol",
   262→        aggregate_id=protocol.id,
   263→        payload={
   264→            "protocol_id": protocol.id,
   265→            "title": protocol.title,
   266→            "file_uri": protocol.file_uri,
   267→        },
   268→    )
   269→    db.commit()
   270→    db.refresh(protocol)
   271→
   272→    return _protocol_to_response(protocol)
   273→
   274→
   275→@router.post("/{protocol_id}/retry", response_model=RetryResponse)
   276→async def retry_protocol(
   277→    protocol_id: str,
   278→    db: Session = Depends(get_db),
   279→) -> RetryResponse:
   280→    """Retry a failed protocol by resuming from the last LangGraph checkpoint.
   281→
   282→    Accepts protocols in extraction_failed, grounding_failed, or dead_letter states.
   283→    Updates the protocol status to processing, then resumes the pipeline from the
   284→    last successful checkpoint (via LangGraph PostgresSaver) — skipping nodes
   285→    that already succeeded (ingest, extract, parse) and retrying from the failed node.
   286→
   287→    This does NOT create a new outbox event. The retry goes directly through
   288→    the graph using the saved checkpoint. Initial pipeline runs still use the
   289→    outbox trigger pattern (handle_protocol_uploaded).
   290→    """
   291→    protocol = db.get(Protocol, protocol_id)
   292→    if not protocol:
   293→        raise HTTPException(
   294→            status_code=404,
   295→            detail=f"Protocol {protocol_id} not found",
   296→        )
   297→
   298→    retryable_states = ["extraction_failed", "grounding_failed", "dead_letter"]
   299→    if protocol.status not in retryable_states:
   300→        raise HTTPException(
   301→            status_code=400,
   302→            detail=f"Protocol is not in a retryable state (current: {protocol.status})",
   303→        )
   304→
   305→    previous_status = protocol.status
   306→    protocol.status = "processing"
   307→    protocol.error_reason = None
   308→    db.add(protocol)
   309→    db.commit()
   310→
   311→    try:
   312→        from protocol_processor.trigger import retry_from_checkpoint
   313→
   314→        await retry_from_checkpoint(protocol_id)
   315→    except Exception as e:
   316→        protocol.status = previous_status
   317→        protocol.error_reason = str(e)[:500]
   318→        db.add(protocol)
   319→        db.commit()
   320→        logger.error("Retry failed for protocol %s: %s", protocol_id, e)
   321→
   322→    return RetryResponse(status="retry_started", protocol_id=protocol_id)
   323→
   324→
   325→@router.post("/{protocol_id}/archive", response_model=ProtocolResponse)
   326→def archive_protocol(
   327→    protocol_id: str,
   328→    db: Session = Depends(get_db),
   329→) -> ProtocolResponse:
   330→    """Archive a failed or dead-letter protocol.
   331→
   332→    Only protocols in dead_letter, extraction_failed, or grounding_failed
   333→    states may be manually archived. Returns 400 for non-archivable states.
   334→    """
   335→    protocol = db.get(Protocol, protocol_id)
   336→    if not protocol:
   337→        raise HTTPException(
   338→            status_code=404,
   339→            detail=f"Protocol {protocol_id} not found",
   340→        )
   341→
   342→    archivable_states = {"dead_letter", "extraction_failed", "grounding_failed"}
   343→    if protocol.status not in archivable_states:
   344→        raise HTTPException(
   345→            status_code=400,
   346→            detail=(
   347→                f"Protocol cannot be archived from state '{protocol.status}'. "
   348→                "Archivable states: " + ", ".join(sorted(archivable_states))
   349→            ),
   350→        )
   351→
   352→    protocol.status = "archived"
   353→    db.add(protocol)
   354→    db.commit()
   355→    db.refresh(protocol)
   356→
   357→    return _protocol_to_response(protocol)
   358→
   359→
   360→@router.post("/{protocol_id}/re-extract", response_model=ReExtractResponse)
   361→def re_extract_protocol(
   362→    protocol_id: str,
   363→    db: Session = Depends(get_db),
   364→) -> ReExtractResponse:
   365→    """Trigger re-extraction on an existing protocol without re-uploading the PDF.
   366→
   367→    Archives all existing non-archived batches for this protocol (preserves them
   368→    in the database but hides them from the dashboard and review page). Collects
   369→    reviewed criteria from the archived batches to enable review inheritance.
   370→    Triggers the extraction pipeline via outbox event (same flow as initial upload).
   371→
   372→    The extraction pipeline will create a new batch. Review decisions from archived
   373→    criteria are auto-inherited for criteria with >90% fuzzy text match (same type).
   374→
   375→    Returns 404 if protocol not found.
   376→    Returns 409 if protocol is in a processing state (extracting, grounding) to
   377→    prevent race conditions — only terminal states are re-extractable.
   378→    """
   379→    protocol = db.get(Protocol, protocol_id)
   380→    if not protocol:
   381→        raise HTTPException(
   382→            status_code=404,
   383→            detail=f"Protocol {protocol_id} not found",
   384→        )
   385→
   386→    # Only allow re-extraction from terminal states — prevent race conditions
   387→    # with an in-progress pipeline run (Pitfall 5 from RESEARCH.md)
   388→    terminal_states = {
   389→        "pending_review",
   390→        "complete",
   391→        "extraction_failed",
   392→        "grounding_failed",
   393→        "dead_letter",
   394→    }
   395→    if protocol.status not in terminal_states:
   396→        raise HTTPException(
   397→            status_code=409,
   398→            detail=(
   399→                f"Protocol is not in a terminal state (current: {protocol.status}). "
   400→                "Re-extraction is only allowed for: "
   401→                + ", ".join(sorted(terminal_states))
   402→            ),
   403→        )
   404→
   405→    # Find all non-archived batches for this protocol
   406→    existing_batches = db.exec(
   407→        select(CriteriaBatch).where(
   408→            CriteriaBatch.protocol_id == protocol_id,
   409→            CriteriaBatch.is_archived == False,  # noqa: E712
   410→        )
   411→    ).all()
   412→
   413→    # Collect reviewed criteria from batches being archived (for inheritance)
   414→    archived_reviewed_criteria: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
   415→    for batch in existing_batches:
   416→        criteria = db.exec(
   417→            select(Criteria).where(
   418→                Criteria.batch_id == batch.id,
   419→                col(Criteria.review_status).isnot(None),
   420→            )
   421→        ).all()
   422→        for criterion in criteria:
   423→            archived_reviewed_criteria.append(
   424→                {
   425→                    "id": criterion.id,
   426→                    "text": criterion.text,
   427→                    "criteria_type": criterion.criteria_type,
   428→                    "review_status": criterion.review_status,
   429→                    "reviewed_by": None,  # Review reviewer_id tracked in Review table
   430→                }
   431→            )
   432→
   433→    # Archive all existing non-archived batches
   434→    archived_count = len(existing_batches)
   435→    for batch in existing_batches:
   436→        batch.is_archived = True
   437→        db.add(batch)
   438→
   439→    logger.info(
   440→        "Archiving %d batch(es) for protocol %s before re-extraction "
   441→        "(collected %d reviewed criteria for inheritance)",
   442→        archived_count,
   443→        protocol_id,
   444→        len(archived_reviewed_criteria),
   445→    )
   446→
   447→    # Update protocol status to extracting and trigger extraction pipeline
   448→    protocol.status = "extracting"
   449→    protocol.error_reason = None
   450→
   451→    # Trigger extraction via outbox event — reuses existing pipeline
   452→    # (same as initial upload flow). Include archived reviewed criteria in
   453→    # payload so the persist node can apply review inheritance after batch creation.
   454→    persist_with_outbox(
   455→        session=db,
   456→        entity=protocol,
   457→        event_type=DomainEventKind.PROTOCOL_UPLOADED,
   458→        aggregate_type="protocol",
   459→        aggregate_id=protocol.id,
   460→        payload={
   461→            "protocol_id": protocol.id,
   462→            "title": protocol.title,
   463→            "file_uri": protocol.file_uri,
   464→            "archived_reviewed_criteria": archived_reviewed_criteria,
   465→        },
   466→    )
   467→
   468→    db.commit()
   469→
   470→    return ReExtractResponse(
   471→        status="extracting",
   472→        protocol_id=protocol_id,
   473→        archived_batches=archived_count,
   474→    )
   475→
   476→
   477→@router.get("", response_model=ProtocolListResponse)
   478→def list_protocols(
   479→    page: int = Query(default=1, ge=1),
   480→    page_size: int = Query(default=20, ge=1, le=100),
   481→    status: str | None = Query(default=None),
   482→    deduplicate: bool = Query(default=False),
   483→    db: Session = Depends(get_db),
   484→) -> ProtocolListResponse:
   485→    """List protocols with pagination and optional status filter.
   486→
   487→    Returns a paginated list sorted by creation date (newest first).
   488→    Includes total count, page number, page size, and total pages.
   489→    Excludes archived protocols from default view unless explicitly filtered.
   490→
   491→    When deduplicate=True, returns one protocol per unique title (the latest
   492→    version) with a version_count field indicating how many versions exist.
   493→    Archived protocols are excluded from deduplication queries.
   494→    """
   495→    if deduplicate:
   496→        return _list_protocols_deduplicated(page, page_size, status, db)
   497→
   498→    # Build count query
   499→    count_stmt = select(func.count()).select_from(Protocol)
   500→    if status:
   501→        count_stmt = count_stmt.where(Protocol.status == status)
   502→    else:
   503→        # Exclude archived protocols from default view
   504→        count_stmt = count_stmt.where(Protocol.status != "archived")
   505→    total = db.exec(count_stmt).one()
   506→
   507→    # Build data query
   508→    data_stmt = select(Protocol)
   509→    if status:
   510→        data_stmt = data_stmt.where(Protocol.status == status)
   511→    else:
   512→        # Exclude archived protocols from default view
   513→        data_stmt = data_stmt.where(Protocol.status != "archived")
   514→    data_stmt = (
   515→        data_stmt.offset((page - 1) * page_size)
   516→        .limit(page_size)
   517→        .order_by(Protocol.created_at.desc())  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
   518→    )
   519→
   520→    protocols = db.exec(data_stmt).all()
   521→    pages = ceil(total / page_size) if total > 0 else 1
   522→
   523→    return ProtocolListResponse(
   524→        items=[_protocol_to_response(p) for p in protocols],
   525→        total=total,
   526→        page=page,
   527→        page_size=page_size,
   528→        pages=pages,
   529→    )
   530→
   531→
   532→def _list_protocols_deduplicated(
   533→    page: int,
   534→    page_size: int,
   535→    status: str | None,
   536→    db: Session,
   537→) -> ProtocolListResponse:
   538→    """Return one protocol per unique title (latest version) with version_count.
   539→
   540→    Excludes archived protocols. When status filter is provided, only titles
   541→    whose latest version matches that status are returned.
   542→    """
   543→    # Subquery: latest created_at per title (excluding archived)
   544→    latest_subq = (
   545→        select(Protocol.title, func.max(Protocol.created_at).label("max_created"))
   546→        .where(Protocol.status != "archived")
   547→        .group_by(Protocol.title)
   548→        .subquery()
   549→    )
   550→
   551→    # Count subquery: number of (non-archived) versions per title
   552→    count_subq = (
   553→        select(Protocol.title, func.count(col(Protocol.id)).label("version_count"))
   554→        .where(Protocol.status != "archived")
   555→        .group_by(Protocol.title)
   556→        .subquery()
   557→    )
   558→
   559→    # Join Protocol to latest_subq to get the canonical (newest) row per title
   560→    dedup_stmt = select(Protocol).join(
   561→        latest_subq,
   562→        (col(Protocol.title) == latest_subq.c.title)
   563→        & (col(Protocol.created_at) == latest_subq.c.max_created),
   564→    )
   565→
   566→    if status:
   567→        dedup_stmt = dedup_stmt.where(Protocol.status == status)
   568→
   569→    # Count total unique titles (for pagination)
   570→    count_stmt = select(func.count()).select_from(dedup_stmt.subquery())
   571→    total = db.exec(count_stmt).one()
   572→
   573→    # Apply pagination and ordering
   574→    dedup_stmt = (
   575→        dedup_stmt.order_by(Protocol.created_at.desc())  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
   576→        .offset((page - 1) * page_size)
   577→        .limit(page_size)
   578→    )
   579→
   580→    protocols = db.exec(dedup_stmt).all()
   581→    pages = ceil(total / page_size) if total > 0 else 1
   582→
   583→    # Build version_count lookup from count subquery
   584→    version_counts: dict[str, int] = {
   585→        row[0]: row[1]
   586→        for row in db.exec(select(count_subq.c.title, count_subq.c.version_count)).all()
   587→    }
   588→
   589→    return ProtocolListResponse(
   590→        items=[
   591→            _protocol_to_response(p, version_count=version_counts.get(p.title, 1))
   592→            for p in protocols
   593→        ],
   594→        total=total,
   595→        page=page,
   596→        page_size=page_size,
   597→        pages=pages,
   598→    )
   599→
   600→
   601→@router.get("/{protocol_id}", response_model=ProtocolResponse)
   602→def get_protocol(
   603→    protocol_id: str,
   604→    db: Session = Depends(get_db),
   605→) -> ProtocolResponse:
   606→    """Get protocol detail including quality score and metadata.
   607→
   608→    Performs lazy archival: if a protocol is in dead_letter status and
   609→    hasn't been updated in 7+ days, it is automatically transitioned to archived.
   610→    """
   611→    protocol = db.get(Protocol, protocol_id)
   612→    if not protocol:
   613→        raise HTTPException(
   614→            status_code=404,
   615→            detail=f"Protocol {protocol_id} not found",
   616→        )
   617→
   618→    # Check for dead-letter archival
   619→    _check_dead_letter_archival(protocol, db)
   620→
   621→    return _protocol_to_response(protocol)
   622→
   623→
   624→@router.get(
   625→    "/{protocol_id}/batches",
   626→    response_model=list[BatchSummaryResponse],
   627→)
   628→def list_protocol_batches(
   629→    protocol_id: str,
   630→    db: Session = Depends(get_db),
   631→) -> list[BatchSummaryResponse]:
   632→    """List ALL batches for a protocol including archived ones.
   633→
   634→    Returns batches in chronological order (oldest first) for the batch
   635→    timeline view. Unlike GET /reviews/batches which excludes archived
   636→    batches for the review workflow, this endpoint includes them so that
   637→    reviewers can see the full re-extraction history.
   638→
   639→    This is intentionally separate from the existing batch listing endpoint
   640→    to preserve the review workflow's correct exclusion of archived batches
   641→    (per research anti-pattern guidance).
   642→
   643→    Returns 404 if protocol doesn't exist.
   644→    """
   645→    # Verify protocol exists
   646→    protocol = db.get(Protocol, protocol_id)
   647→    if not protocol:
   648→        raise HTTPException(
   649→            status_code=404,
   650→            detail=f"Protocol {protocol_id} not found",
   651→        )
   652→
   653→    # Load ALL batches for this protocol (no is_archived filter)
   654→    # ordered chronologically ascending for timeline display
   655→    batches = db.exec(
   656→        select(CriteriaBatch)
   657→        .where(CriteriaBatch.protocol_id == protocol_id)
   658→        .order_by(col(CriteriaBatch.created_at).asc())
   659→    ).all()
   660→
   661→    if not batches:
   662→        return []
   663→
   664→    # Batch-load criteria counts to avoid N+1 queries
   665→    batch_ids = [b.id for b in batches]
   666→
   667→    # Total criteria count per batch
   668→    total_counts_stmt = (
   669→        select(Criteria.batch_id, func.count(col(Criteria.id)).label("cnt"))
   670→        .where(col(Criteria.batch_id).in_(batch_ids))
   671→        .group_by(Criteria.batch_id)
   672→    )
   673→    total_counts: dict[str, int] = {
   674→        row[0]: row[1] for row in db.exec(total_counts_stmt).all()
   675→    }
   676→
   677→    # Reviewed criteria count per batch (review_status IS NOT NULL)
   678→    reviewed_counts_stmt = (
   679→        select(Criteria.batch_id, func.count(col(Criteria.id)).label("cnt"))
   680→        .where(
   681→            col(Criteria.batch_id).in_(batch_ids),
   682→            col(Criteria.review_status).isnot(None),
   683→        )
   684→        .group_by(Criteria.batch_id)
   685→    )
   686→    reviewed_counts: dict[str, int] = {
   687→        row[0]: row[1] for row in db.exec(reviewed_counts_stmt).all()
   688→    }
   689→
   690→    return [
   691→        BatchSummaryResponse(
   692→            id=batch.id,
   693→            protocol_id=batch.protocol_id,
   694→            status=batch.status,
   695→            is_archived=batch.is_archived,
   696→            criteria_count=total_counts.get(batch.id, 0),
   697→            reviewed_count=reviewed_counts.get(batch.id, 0),
   698→            extraction_model=batch.extraction_model,
   699→            created_at=batch.created_at,
   700→        )
   701→        for batch in batches
   702→    ]
   703→
   704→
   705→# --- Helpers ---
   706→
   707→
   708→def _protocol_to_response(
   709→    protocol: Protocol, version_count: int = 1
   710→) -> ProtocolResponse:
   711→    """Convert a Protocol model to ProtocolResponse."""
   712→    return ProtocolResponse(
   713→        id=protocol.id,
   714→        title=protocol.title,
   715→        file_uri=protocol.file_uri,
   716→        status=protocol.status,
   717→        page_count=protocol.page_count,
   718→        quality_score=protocol.quality_score,
   719→        metadata_=protocol.metadata_,
   720→        created_at=protocol.created_at,
   721→        updated_at=protocol.updated_at,
   722→        error_reason=protocol.error_reason,
   723→        version_count=version_count,
   724→    )
   725→
   726→
   727→def _check_dead_letter_archival(protocol: Protocol, session: Session) -> None:
   728→    """Check if a dead-letter protocol should be archived.
   729→
   730→    Archives protocols that have been in dead_letter status for more than 7 days
   731→    since their last update. This is a lazy archival strategy triggered on access.
   732→    """
   733→    if protocol.status != "dead_letter":
   734→        return
   735→
   736→    # Check if updated_at is more than 7 days ago
   737→    cutoff = datetime.now(timezone.utc) - timedelta(days=7)
   738→    if protocol.updated_at < cutoff:
   739→        logger.info(
   740→            "Archiving dead-letter protocol %s (last updated: %s)",
   741→            protocol.id,
   742→            protocol.updated_at,
   743→        )
   744→        protocol.status = "archived"
   745→        session.add(protocol)
   746→        session.commit()
   747→
   748→
   749→def _apply_review_inheritance(
   750→    db: Session,
   751→    protocol_id: str,
   752→    archived_criteria: list[dict[str, Any]],
   753→) -> None:
   754→    """Auto-inherit review decisions from archived criteria to new batch criteria.
   755→
   756→    Finds the newest non-archived batch for the protocol, then uses fuzzy
   757→    matching to identify matching criteria from the archived set. For each
   758→    match above the 90% threshold (same criteria_type), copies the review
   759→    decision to the new criterion and creates a Review record.
   760→
   761→    Called from the extraction pipeline after new batch/criteria are persisted
   762→    (e.g., from persist_node in extraction-service when archived_reviewed_criteria
   763→    is present in the outbox payload).
   764→
   765→    Args:
   766→        db: Database session.
   767→        protocol_id: Protocol to apply inheritance for.
   768→        archived_criteria: List of criterion dicts from archived batches.
   769→            Must have: id, text, criteria_type, review_status.
   770→    """
   771→    if not archived_criteria:
   772→        return
   773→
   774→    # Find the newest non-archived batch for this protocol
   775→    newest_batch = db.exec(
   776→        select(CriteriaBatch)
   777→        .where(
   778→            CriteriaBatch.protocol_id == protocol_id,
   779→            CriteriaBatch.is_archived == False,  # noqa: E712
   780→        )
   781→        .order_by(col(CriteriaBatch.created_at).desc())
   782→        .limit(1)
   783→    ).first()
   784→
   785→    if not newest_batch:
   786→        logger.warning(
   787→            "No non-archived batch found for protocol %s — skipping inheritance",
   788→            protocol_id,
   789→        )
   790→        return
   791→
   792→    # Load new criteria from the newest batch
   793→    new_criteria_list = db.exec(
   794→        select(Criteria).where(Criteria.batch_id == newest_batch.id)
   795→    ).all()
   796→
   797→    if not new_criteria_list:
   798→        return
   799→
   800→    # Convert new criteria to dicts for fuzzy matching
   801→    new_criteria_dicts = [
   802→        {
   803→            "id": c.id,
   804→            "text": c.text,
   805→            "criteria_type": c.criteria_type,
   806→        }
   807→        for c in new_criteria_list
   808→    ]
   809→
   810→    # Run fuzzy matching to find inheritance candidates
   811→    matches = inherit_reviews_for_batch(
   812→        new_criteria=new_criteria_dicts,
   813→        archived_criteria=archived_criteria,
   814→    )
   815→
   816→    if not matches:
   817→        logger.info(
   818→            "No review decisions inherited for protocol %s (0 matches found)",
   819→            protocol_id,
   820→        )
   821→        return
   822→
   823→    # Build lookup for fast criterion access
   824→    criteria_by_id = {c.id: c for c in new_criteria_list}
   825→
   826→    # Apply inheritance: update criterion status and create Review records
   827→    inherited = 0
   828→    for match in matches:
   829→        criterion = criteria_by_id.get(match["new_criterion_id"])
   830→        if not criterion:
   831→            continue
   832→
   833→        criterion.review_status = match["review_status"]
   834→        db.add(criterion)
   835→
   836→        # Create Review record documenting the auto-inheritance
   837→        review = Review(
   838→            reviewer_id="system:re-extraction-inheritance",
   839→            target_type="criteria",
   840→            target_id=criterion.id,
   841→            action=match["review_status"],  # e.g., "approved", "rejected", "modified"
   842→            before_value={"review_status": None},
   843→            after_value={
   844→                "review_status": match["review_status"],
   845→                "inherited_from": match["old_criterion_id"],
   846→                "match_score": match["match_score"],
   847→            },
   848→            comment=(
   849→                f"Auto-inherited from archived criterion {match['old_criterion_id']} "
   850→                f"(fuzzy match score: {match['match_score']:.1f}%)"
   851→            ),
   852→        )
   853→        db.add(review)
   854→        inherited += 1
   855→
   856→    db.commit()
   857→
   858→    logger.info(
   859→        "Inherited %d/%d review decisions for protocol %s",
   860→        inherited,
   861→        len(new_criteria_list),
   862→        protocol_id,
   863→    )
   864→
```

     1→"""FastAPI router for protocol upload, confirmation, listing, and detail.
     2→
     3→Provides endpoints for:
     4→- POST /protocols/upload: Generate signed URL for direct browser-to-GCS upload
     5→- POST /protocols/{id}/confirm-upload: Confirm and quality-check
     6→- GET /protocols: Paginated protocol list with optional status filter
     7→- GET /protocols/{protocol_id}: Protocol detail view with quality metadata
     8→- POST /protocols/{protocol_id}/retry: Resume failed protocol from LangGraph checkpoint
     9→- GET /protocols/{protocol_id}/batches: All batches including archived (timeline)
    10→"""
    11→

    15→import logging

    43→
    44→# --- Request/Response models ---
    45→
    46→
