   131→# --- Endpoints ---
   132→
   133→
   134→@router.post("/upload", response_model=UploadResponse)
   135→def upload_protocol(
   136→    body: UploadRequest,
   137→    db: Session = Depends(get_db),
   138→) -> UploadResponse:
   139→    """Generate a signed URL for uploading a protocol PDF.
   140→
   141→    Validates the content type (must be PDF) and file size (max 50MB),
   142→    creates a Protocol record in the database, and returns a signed
   143→    URL for direct browser upload to GCS.
   144→    """
   145→    # Validate content type
   146→    if body.content_type != "application/pdf":
   147→        raise HTTPException(
   148→            status_code=400,
   149→            detail="Only PDF files are accepted",
   150→        )
   151→
   152→    # Validate file size (50MB limit)
   153→    max_size = 50 * 1024 * 1024
   154→    if body.file_size_bytes > max_size:
   155→        raise HTTPException(
   156→            status_code=400,
   157→            detail="File size exceeds 50MB limit",
   158→        )
   159→
   160→    # Generate signed upload URL
   161→    signed_url, gcs_path = generate_upload_url(
   162→        filename=body.filename,
   163→        content_type=body.content_type,
   164→    )
   165→
   166→    # Create protocol record with title from filename (strip .pdf)
   167→    title = body.filename
   168→    if title.lower().endswith(".pdf"):
   169→        title = title[:-4]
   170→
   171→    protocol = Protocol(
   172→        title=title,
   173→        file_uri=gcs_path,
   174→        status="uploaded",
   175→    )
   176→
   177→    # Persist protocol + outbox event atomically
   178→    persist_with_outbox(
   179→        session=db,
   180→        entity=protocol,
   181→        event_type=DomainEventKind.PROTOCOL_UPLOADED,
   182→        aggregate_type="protocol",
   183→        aggregate_id=protocol.id,
   184→        payload={
   185→            "protocol_id": protocol.id,
   186→            "title": title,
   187→            "file_uri": gcs_path,
   188→        },
   189→    )
   190→    db.commit()
   191→
   192→    # Check circuit breaker states — if critical services are down,
   193→    # mark protocol as pending with warning (per locked decision)
   194→    processing_warning: str | None = None
   195→    if gemini_breaker.current_state != "closed":
   196→        protocol.status = "pending"
   197→        processing_warning = "Processing delayed — AI service temporarily unavailable"
   198→        protocol.metadata_ = {
   199→            **protocol.metadata_,
   200→            "processing_warning": processing_warning,
   201→        }
   202→        db.add(protocol)
   203→        db.commit()
   204→
   205→    return UploadResponse(
   206→        protocol_id=protocol.id,
   207→        upload_url=signed_url,
   208→        gcs_path=gcs_path,
   209→        warning=processing_warning,
   210→    )
   211→
   212→
   213→@router.post(
   214→    "/{protocol_id}/confirm-upload",
   215→    response_model=ProtocolResponse,
   216→)
   217→def confirm_upload(
   218→    protocol_id: str,
   219→    body: ConfirmUploadRequest,
   220→    db: Session = Depends(get_db),
   221→) -> ProtocolResponse:
   222→    """Confirm a protocol upload and optionally trigger quality analysis.
   223→
   224→    Looks up the protocol by ID. If pdf_bytes_base64 is provided,
   225→    decodes the PDF bytes and computes a quality score including
   226→    text extractability, page count, and encoding type.
   227→    """
   228→    protocol = db.get(Protocol, protocol_id)
   229→    if not protocol:
   230→        raise HTTPException(
   231→            status_code=404,
   232→            detail=f"Protocol {protocol_id} not found",
   233→        )
   234→
   235→    if body.pdf_bytes_base64:
   236→        try:
   237→            pdf_bytes = base64.b64decode(body.pdf_bytes_base64)
   238→            result = compute_quality_score(pdf_bytes)
   239→
   240→            protocol.quality_score = result.overall_score
   241→            protocol.page_count = result.page_count
   242→            protocol.metadata_ = {"quality": result.model_dump()}
   243→
   244→            # Store quality metadata on GCS blob (best-effort)
   245→            try:
   246→                set_blob_metadata(
   247→                    protocol.file_uri,
   248→                    quality_result_to_metadata(result),
   249→                )
   250→            except Exception:
   251→                logger.warning(
   252→                    "Failed to set GCS metadata for %s",
   253→                    protocol.file_uri,
   254→                    exc_info=True,
   255→                )
   256→        except Exception:
   257→            logger.warning(
   258→                "Failed to compute quality score for protocol %s",
   259→                protocol_id,
   260→                exc_info=True,
   261→            )
   262→
   263→    db.add(protocol)
   264→    db.commit()
   265→    db.refresh(protocol)
   266→
   267→    return _protocol_to_response(protocol)
   268→
   269→
   270→@router.post("/{protocol_id}/retry", response_model=RetryResponse)
   271→async def retry_protocol(
   272→    protocol_id: str,
   273→    db: Session = Depends(get_db),
   274→) -> RetryResponse:
   275→    """Retry a failed protocol by resuming from the last LangGraph checkpoint.
   276→
   277→    Accepts protocols in extraction_failed, grounding_failed, or dead_letter states.
   278→    Updates the protocol status to processing, then resumes the pipeline from the
   279→    last successful checkpoint (via LangGraph PostgresSaver) — skipping nodes
   280→    that already succeeded (ingest, extract, parse) and retrying from the failed node.
   281→
   282→    This does NOT create a new outbox event. The retry goes directly through
   283→    the graph using the saved checkpoint. Initial pipeline runs still use the
   284→    outbox trigger pattern (handle_protocol_uploaded).
   285→    """
   286→    protocol = db.get(Protocol, protocol_id)
   287→    if not protocol:
   288→        raise HTTPException(
   289→            status_code=404,
   290→            detail=f"Protocol {protocol_id} not found",
   291→        )
   292→
   293→    retryable_states = ["extraction_failed", "grounding_failed", "dead_letter"]
   294→    if protocol.status not in retryable_states:
   295→        raise HTTPException(
   296→            status_code=400,
   297→            detail=f"Protocol is not in a retryable state (current: {protocol.status})",
   298→        )
   299→
   300→    # Update status to processing
   301→    protocol.status = "processing"
   302→    protocol.error_reason = None
   303→    db.add(protocol)
   304→    db.commit()
   305→
   306→    # Resume from checkpoint — await directly (no asyncio.run inside async endpoints)
   307→    try:
   308→        from protocol_processor.trigger import retry_from_checkpoint
   309→
   310→        await retry_from_checkpoint(protocol_id)
   311→    except Exception as e:
   312→        protocol.error_reason = str(e)[:500]
   313→        db.add(protocol)
   314→        db.commit()
   315→        logger.error("Retry failed for protocol %s: %s", protocol_id, e)
   316→
   317→    return RetryResponse(status="retry_started", protocol_id=protocol_id)
   318→
   319→
   320→@router.post("/{protocol_id}/re-extract", response_model=ReExtractResponse)
   321→def re_extract_protocol(
   322→    protocol_id: str,
   323→    db: Session = Depends(get_db),
   324→) -> ReExtractResponse:
   325→    """Trigger re-extraction on an existing protocol without re-uploading the PDF.
   326→
   327→    Archives all existing non-archived batches for this protocol (preserves them
   328→    in the database but hides them from the dashboard and review page). Collects
   329→    reviewed criteria from the archived batches to enable review inheritance.
   330→    Triggers the extraction pipeline via outbox event (same flow as initial upload).
   331→
   332→    The extraction pipeline will create a new batch. Review decisions from archived
   333→    criteria are auto-inherited for criteria with >90% fuzzy text match (same type).
   334→
   335→    Returns 404 if protocol not found.
   336→    Returns 409 if protocol is in a processing state (extracting, grounding) to
   337→    prevent race conditions — only terminal states are re-extractable.
   338→    """
   339→    protocol = db.get(Protocol, protocol_id)
   340→    if not protocol:
   341→        raise HTTPException(
   342→            status_code=404,
   343→            detail=f"Protocol {protocol_id} not found",
   344→        )
   345→
   346→    # Only allow re-extraction from terminal states — prevent race conditions
   347→    # with an in-progress pipeline run (Pitfall 5 from RESEARCH.md)
   348→    terminal_states = {
   349→        "pending_review",
   350→        "complete",
   351→        "extraction_failed",
   352→        "grounding_failed",
   353→        "dead_letter",
   354→    }
   355→    if protocol.status not in terminal_states:
   356→        raise HTTPException(
   357→            status_code=409,
   358→            detail=(
   359→                f"Protocol is not in a terminal state (current: {protocol.status}). "
   360→                "Re-extraction is only allowed for: "
   361→                + ", ".join(sorted(terminal_states))
   362→            ),
   363→        )
   364→
   365→    # Find all non-archived batches for this protocol
   366→    existing_batches = db.exec(
   367→        select(CriteriaBatch).where(
   368→            CriteriaBatch.protocol_id == protocol_id,
   369→            CriteriaBatch.is_archived == False,  # noqa: E712
   370→        )
   371→    ).all()
   372→
   373→    # Collect reviewed criteria from batches being archived (for inheritance)
   374→    archived_reviewed_criteria: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
   375→    for batch in existing_batches:
   376→        criteria = db.exec(
   377→            select(Criteria).where(
   378→                Criteria.batch_id == batch.id,
   379→                col(Criteria.review_status).isnot(None),
   380→            )
   381→        ).all()
   382→        for criterion in criteria:
   383→            archived_reviewed_criteria.append(
   384→                {
   385→                    "id": criterion.id,
   386→                    "text": criterion.text,
   387→                    "criteria_type": criterion.criteria_type,
   388→                    "review_status": criterion.review_status,
   389→                    "reviewed_by": None,  # Review reviewer_id tracked in Review table
   390→                }
   391→            )
   392→
   393→    # Archive all existing non-archived batches
   394→    archived_count = len(existing_batches)
   395→    for batch in existing_batches:
   396→        batch.is_archived = True
   397→        db.add(batch)
   398→
   399→    logger.info(
   400→        "Archiving %d batch(es) for protocol %s before re-extraction "
   401→        "(collected %d reviewed criteria for inheritance)",
   402→        archived_count,
   403→        protocol_id,
   404→        len(archived_reviewed_criteria),
   405→    )
   406→
   407→    # Update protocol status to extracting and trigger extraction pipeline
   408→    protocol.status = "extracting"
   409→    protocol.error_reason = None
   410→
   411→    # Trigger extraction via outbox event — reuses existing pipeline
   412→    # (same as initial upload flow). Include archived reviewed criteria in
   413→    # payload so the persist node can apply review inheritance after batch creation.
   414→    persist_with_outbox(
   415→        session=db,
   416→        entity=protocol,
   417→        event_type=DomainEventKind.PROTOCOL_UPLOADED,
   418→        aggregate_type="protocol",
   419→        aggregate_id=protocol.id,
   420→        payload={
   421→            "protocol_id": protocol.id,
   422→            "title": protocol.title,
   423→            "file_uri": protocol.file_uri,
   424→            "archived_reviewed_criteria": archived_reviewed_criteria,
   425→        },
   426→    )
   427→
   428→    db.commit()
   429→
   430→    return ReExtractResponse(
   431→        status="extracting",
   432→        protocol_id=protocol_id,
   433→        archived_batches=archived_count,
   434→    )
   435→
   436→
   437→@router.get("", response_model=ProtocolListResponse)
   438→def list_protocols(
   439→    page: int = Query(default=1, ge=1),
   440→    page_size: int = Query(default=20, ge=1, le=100),
   441→    status: str | None = Query(default=None),
   442→    db: Session = Depends(get_db),
   443→) -> ProtocolListResponse:
   444→    """List protocols with pagination and optional status filter.
   445→
   446→    Returns a paginated list sorted by creation date (newest first).
   447→    Includes total count, page number, page size, and total pages.
   448→    Excludes archived protocols from default view unless explicitly filtered.
   449→    """
   450→    # Build count query
   451→    count_stmt = select(func.count()).select_from(Protocol)
   452→    if status:
   453→        count_stmt = count_stmt.where(Protocol.status == status)
   454→    else:
   455→        # Exclude archived protocols from default view
   456→        count_stmt = count_stmt.where(Protocol.status != "archived")
   457→    total = db.exec(count_stmt).one()
   458→
   459→    # Build data query
   460→    data_stmt = select(Protocol)
   461→    if status:
   462→        data_stmt = data_stmt.where(Protocol.status == status)
   463→    else:
   464→        # Exclude archived protocols from default view
   465→        data_stmt = data_stmt.where(Protocol.status != "archived")
   466→    data_stmt = (
   467→        data_stmt.offset((page - 1) * page_size)
   468→        .limit(page_size)
   469→        .order_by(Protocol.created_at.desc())  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
   470→    )
   471→
   472→    protocols = db.exec(data_stmt).all()
   473→    pages = ceil(total / page_size) if total > 0 else 1
   474→
   475→    return ProtocolListResponse(
   476→        items=[_protocol_to_response(p) for p in protocols],
   477→        total=total,
   478→        page=page,
   479→        page_size=page_size,
   480→        pages=pages,
   481→    )
   482→
   483→
   484→@router.get("/{protocol_id}", response_model=ProtocolResponse)
   485→def get_protocol(
   486→    protocol_id: str,
   487→    db: Session = Depends(get_db),
   488→) -> ProtocolResponse:
   489→    """Get protocol detail including quality score and metadata.
   490→
   491→    Performs lazy archival: if a protocol is in dead_letter status and
   492→    hasn't been updated in 7+ days, it is automatically transitioned to archived.
   493→    """
   494→    protocol = db.get(Protocol, protocol_id)
   495→    if not protocol:
   496→        raise HTTPException(
   497→            status_code=404,
   498→            detail=f"Protocol {protocol_id} not found",
   499→        )
   500→
   501→    # Check for dead-letter archival
   502→    _check_dead_letter_archival(protocol, db)
   503→
   504→    return _protocol_to_response(protocol)
   505→
   506→
   507→@router.get(
   508→    "/{protocol_id}/batches",
   509→    response_model=list[BatchSummaryResponse],
   510→)
   511→def list_protocol_batches(
   512→    protocol_id: str,
   513→    db: Session = Depends(get_db),
   514→) -> list[BatchSummaryResponse]:
   515→    """List ALL batches for a protocol including archived ones.
   516→
   517→    Returns batches in chronological order (oldest first) for the batch
   518→    timeline view. Unlike GET /reviews/batches which excludes archived
   519→    batches for the review workflow, this endpoint includes them so that
   520→    reviewers can see the full re-extraction history.
   521→
   522→    This is intentionally separate from the existing batch listing endpoint
   523→    to preserve the review workflow's correct exclusion of archived batches
   524→    (per research anti-pattern guidance).
   525→
   526→    Returns 404 if protocol doesn't exist.
   527→    """
   528→    # Verify protocol exists
   529→    protocol = db.get(Protocol, protocol_id)
   530→    if not protocol:
   531→        raise HTTPException(
   532→            status_code=404,
   533→            detail=f"Protocol {protocol_id} not found",
   534→        )
   535→
   536→    # Load ALL batches for this protocol (no is_archived filter)
   537→    # ordered chronologically ascending for timeline display
   538→    batches = db.exec(
   539→        select(CriteriaBatch)
   540→        .where(CriteriaBatch.protocol_id == protocol_id)
   541→        .order_by(col(CriteriaBatch.created_at).asc())
   542→    ).all()
   543→
   544→    if not batches:
   545→        return []
   546→
   547→    # Batch-load criteria counts to avoid N+1 queries
   548→    batch_ids = [b.id for b in batches]
   549→
   550→    # Total criteria count per batch
   551→    total_counts_stmt = (
   552→        select(Criteria.batch_id, func.count(Criteria.id).label("cnt"))  # type: ignore[arg-type]
   553→        .where(col(Criteria.batch_id).in_(batch_ids))
   554→        .group_by(Criteria.batch_id)
   555→    )
   556→    total_counts: dict[str, int] = {
   557→        row[0]: row[1] for row in db.exec(total_counts_stmt).all()
   558→    }
   559→
   560→    # Reviewed criteria count per batch (review_status IS NOT NULL)
   561→    reviewed_counts_stmt = (
   562→        select(Criteria.batch_id, func.count(Criteria.id).label("cnt"))  # type: ignore[arg-type]
   563→        .where(
   564→            col(Criteria.batch_id).in_(batch_ids),
   565→            col(Criteria.review_status).isnot(None),
   566→        )
   567→        .group_by(Criteria.batch_id)
   568→    )
   569→    reviewed_counts: dict[str, int] = {
   570→        row[0]: row[1] for row in db.exec(reviewed_counts_stmt).all()
   571→    }
   572→
   573→    return [
   574→        BatchSummaryResponse(
   575→            id=batch.id,
   576→            protocol_id=batch.protocol_id,
   577→            status=batch.status,
   578→            is_archived=batch.is_archived,
   579→            criteria_count=total_counts.get(batch.id, 0),
   580→            reviewed_count=reviewed_counts.get(batch.id, 0),
   581→            extraction_model=batch.extraction_model,
   582→            created_at=batch.created_at,
   583→        )
   584→        for batch in batches
   585→    ]
   586→
   587→
   588→# --- Helpers ---
   589→
   590→
   591→def _protocol_to_response(protocol: Protocol) -> ProtocolResponse:
   592→    """Convert a Protocol model to ProtocolResponse."""
   593→    return ProtocolResponse(
   594→        id=protocol.id,
   595→        title=protocol.title,
   596→        file_uri=protocol.file_uri,
   597→        status=protocol.status,
   598→        page_count=protocol.page_count,
   599→        quality_score=protocol.quality_score,
   600→        metadata_=protocol.metadata_,
   601→        created_at=protocol.created_at,
   602→        updated_at=protocol.updated_at,
   603→    )
   604→
   605→
   606→def _check_dead_letter_archival(protocol: Protocol, session: Session) -> None:
   607→    """Check if a dead-letter protocol should be archived.
   608→
   609→    Archives protocols that have been in dead_letter status for more than 7 days
   610→    since their last update. This is a lazy archival strategy triggered on access.
   611→    """
   612→    if protocol.status != "dead_letter":
   613→        return
   614→
   615→    # Check if updated_at is more than 7 days ago
   616→    cutoff = datetime.now(timezone.utc) - timedelta(days=7)
   617→    if protocol.updated_at < cutoff:
   618→        logger.info(
   619→            "Archiving dead-letter protocol %s (last updated: %s)",
   620→            protocol.id,
   621→            protocol.updated_at,
   622→        )
   623→        protocol.status = "archived"
   624→        session.add(protocol)
   625→        session.commit()
   626→
   627→
   628→def _apply_review_inheritance(
   629→    db: Session,
   630→    protocol_id: str,
   631→    archived_criteria: list[dict[str, Any]],
   632→) -> None:
   633→    """Auto-inherit review decisions from archived criteria to new batch criteria.
   634→
   635→    Finds the newest non-archived batch for the protocol, then uses fuzzy
   636→    matching to identify matching criteria from the archived set. For each
   637→    match above the 90% threshold (same criteria_type), copies the review
   638→    decision to the new criterion and creates a Review record.
   639→
   640→    Called from the extraction pipeline after new batch/criteria are persisted
   641→    (e.g., from persist_node in extraction-service when archived_reviewed_criteria
   642→    is present in the outbox payload).
   643→
   644→    Args:
   645→        db: Database session.
   646→        protocol_id: Protocol to apply inheritance for.
   647→        archived_criteria: List of criterion dicts from archived batches.
   648→            Must have: id, text, criteria_type, review_status.
   649→    """
   650→    if not archived_criteria:
   651→        return
   652→
   653→    # Find the newest non-archived batch for this protocol
   654→    newest_batch = db.exec(
   655→        select(CriteriaBatch)
   656→        .where(
   657→            CriteriaBatch.protocol_id == protocol_id,
   658→            CriteriaBatch.is_archived == False,  # noqa: E712
   659→        )
   660→        .order_by(col(CriteriaBatch.created_at).desc())
   661→        .limit(1)
   662→    ).first()
   663→
   664→    if not newest_batch:
   665→        logger.warning(
   666→            "No non-archived batch found for protocol %s — skipping inheritance",
   667→            protocol_id,
   668→        )
   669→        return
   670→
   671→    # Load new criteria from the newest batch
   672→    new_criteria_list = db.exec(
   673→        select(Criteria).where(Criteria.batch_id == newest_batch.id)
   674→    ).all()
   675→
   676→    if not new_criteria_list:
   677→        return
   678→
   679→    # Convert new criteria to dicts for fuzzy matching
   680→    new_criteria_dicts = [
   681→        {
   682→            "id": c.id,
   683→            "text": c.text,
   684→            "criteria_type": c.criteria_type,
   685→        }
   686→        for c in new_criteria_list
   687→    ]
   688→
   689→    # Run fuzzy matching to find inheritance candidates
   690→    matches = inherit_reviews_for_batch(
   691→        new_criteria=new_criteria_dicts,
   692→        archived_criteria=archived_criteria,
   693→    )
   694→
   695→    if not matches:
   696→        logger.info(
   697→            "No review decisions inherited for protocol %s (0 matches found)",
   698→            protocol_id,
   699→        )
   700→        return
   701→
   702→    # Build lookup for fast criterion access
   703→    criteria_by_id = {c.id: c for c in new_criteria_list}
   704→
   705→    # Apply inheritance: update criterion status and create Review records
   706→    inherited = 0
   707→    for match in matches:
   708→        criterion = criteria_by_id.get(match["new_criterion_id"])
   709→        if not criterion:
   710→            continue
   711→
   712→        criterion.review_status = match["review_status"]
   713→        db.add(criterion)
   714→
   715→        # Create Review record documenting the auto-inheritance
   716→        review = Review(
   717→            reviewer_id="system:re-extraction-inheritance",
   718→            target_type="criteria",
   719→            target_id=criterion.id,
   720→            action=match["review_status"],  # e.g., "approved", "rejected", "modified"
   721→            before_value={"review_status": None},
   722→            after_value={
   723→                "review_status": match["review_status"],
   724→                "inherited_from": match["old_criterion_id"],
   725→                "match_score": match["match_score"],
   726→            },
   727→            comment=(
   728→                f"Auto-inherited from archived criterion {match['old_criterion_id']} "
   729→                f"(fuzzy match score: {match['match_score']:.1f}%)"
   730→            ),
   731→        )
   732→        db.add(review)
   733→        inherited += 1
   734→
   735→    db.commit()
   736→
   737→    logger.info(
   738→        "Inherited %d/%d review decisions for protocol %s",
   739→        inherited,
   740→        len(new_criteria_list),
   741→        protocol_id,
   742→    )
   743→
```

Validate content type\n    if body.content_type != \"application/pdf\":\n        raise HTTPException(\n            status_code=400,\n            detail=\"Only PDF files are accepted\",\n        )\n\n    # Validate file size (50MB limit)\n    max_size = 50 * 1024 * 1024\n    if body.file_size_bytes > max_size:\n        raise HTTPException(\n            status_code=400,\n            detail=\"File size exceeds 50MB limit\",\n        )\n\n    # Generate signed upload URL\n    signed_url, gcs_path = generate_upload_url(\n        filename=body.filename,\n        content_type=body.content_type,\n    )\n\n    # Create protocol record with title from filename (strip .pdf)\n    title = body.filename\n    if title.lower().endswith(\".pdf\"):\n        title = title[:-4]\n\n    protocol = Protocol(\n        title=title,\n        file_uri=gcs_path,\n        status=\"uploaded\",\n    )\n\n    # Persist protocol record only — the ProtocolUploaded event is deferred\n    # to confirm-upload so the file actually exists when the processor runs.\n    db.add(protocol)\n    db.commit()\n\n    # Check circuit breaker states — if critical services are down,\n    # mark protocol as pending with warning (per locked decision)\n    processing_warning: str | None = None\n    if gemini_breaker.current_state != \"closed\":\n        protocol.status = \"pending\"\n        processing_warning = \"Processing delayed — AI service temporarily unavailable\"\n        protocol.metadata_ = {\n            **protocol.metadata_,\n            \"processing_warning\": processing_warning,\n        }\n        db.add(protocol)\n        db.commit()\n\n    return UploadResponse(\n        protocol_id=protocol.id,\n        upload_url=signed_url,\n        gcs_path=gcs_path,\n        warning=processing_warning,\n    )\n\n\n@router.post(\n    \"/{protocol_id}/confirm-upload\",\n    response_model=ProtocolResponse,\n)\ndef confirm_upload(\n    protocol_id: str,\n    body: ConfirmUploadRequest,\n    db: Session = Depends(get_db),\n) -> ProtocolResponse:\n    \"\"\"Confirm a protocol upload and optionally trigger quality analysis.\n\n    Looks up the protocol by ID. If pdf_bytes_base64 is provided,\n    decodes the PDF bytes and computes a quality score including\n    text extractability, page count, and encoding type.\n    \"\"\"\n    protocol = db.get(Protocol, protocol_id)\n    if not protocol:\n        raise HTTPException(\n            status_code=404,\n            detail=f\"Protocol {protocol_id} not found\",\n        )\n\n    if body.pdf_bytes_base64:\n        try:\n            pdf_bytes = base64.b64decode(body.pdf_bytes_base64)\n            result = compute_quality_score(pdf_bytes)\n\n            protocol.quality_score = result.overall_score\n            protocol.page_count = result.page_count\n            protocol.metadata_ = {\"quality\": result.model_dump()}\n\n            # Store quality metadata on GCS blob (best-effort)\n            try:\n                set_blob_metadata(\n                    protocol.file_uri,\n                    quality_result_to_metadata(result),\n                )\n            except Exception:\n                logger.warning(\n                    \"Failed to set GCS metadata for %s\",\n                    protocol.file_uri,\n                    exc_info=True,\n                )\n        except Exception:\n            logger.warning(\n                \"Failed to compute quality score for protocol %s\",\n                protocol_id,\n                exc_info=True,\n            )\n\n    # Now that the file is confirmed uploaded, publish the outbox event\n    # to trigger the protocol processor pipeline.\n    persist_with_outbox(\n        session=db,\n        entity=protocol,\n        event_type=DomainEventKind.PROTOCOL_UPLOADED,\n        aggregate_type=\"protocol\",\n        aggregate_id=protocol.id,\n        payload={\n            \"protocol_id\": protocol.id,\n            \"title\": protocol.title,\n            \"file_uri\": protocol.file_uri,\n        },\n    )\n    db.commit()\n    db.refresh(protocol)\n\n    return _protocol_to_response(protocol)\n\n\n@router.post(\"/{protocol_id}/retry\", response_model=RetryResponse)\nasync def retry_protocol(\n    protocol_id: str,\n    db: Session = Depends(get_db),\n) -> RetryResponse:\n    \"\"\"Retry a failed protocol by resuming from the last LangGraph checkpoint.\n\n    Accepts protocols in extraction_failed, grounding_failed, or dead_letter states.\n    Updates the protocol status to processing, then resumes the pipeline from the\n    last successful checkpoint (via LangGraph PostgresSaver) — skipping nodes\n    that already succeeded (ingest, extract, parse) and retrying from the failed node.\n\n    This does NOT create a new outbox event. The retry goes directly through\n    the graph using the saved checkpoint. Initial pipeline runs still use the\n    outbox trigger pattern (handle_protocol_uploaded).\n    \"\"\"\n    protocol = db.get(Protocol, protocol_id)\n    if not protocol:\n        raise HTTPException(\n            status_code=404,\n            detail=f\"Protocol {protocol_id} not found\",\n        )\n\n    retryable_states = [\"extraction_failed\", \"grounding_failed\", \"dead_letter\"]\n    if protocol.status not in retryable_states:\n        raise HTTPException(\n            status_code=400,\n            detail=f\"Protocol is not in a retryable state (current: {protocol.status})\",\n        )\n\n    previous_status = protocol.status\n    protocol.status = \"processing\"\n    protocol.error_reason = None\n    db.add(protocol)\n    db.commit()\n\n    try:\n        from protocol_processor.trigger import retry_from_checkpoint\n\n        await retry_from_checkpoint(protocol_id)\n    except Exception as e:\n        protocol.status = previous_status\n        protocol.error_reason = str(e)[:500]\n        db.add(protocol)\n        db.commit()\n        logger.error(\"Retry failed for protocol %s: %s\", protocol_id, e)\n\n    return RetryResponse(status=\"retry_started\", protocol_id=protocol_id)\n\n\n@router.post(\"/{protocol_id}/archive\", response_model=ProtocolResponse)\ndef archive_protocol(\n    protocol_id: str,\n    db: Session = Depends(get_db),\n) -> ProtocolResponse:\n    \"\"\"Archive a failed or dead-letter protocol.\n\n    Only protocols in dead_letter, extraction_failed, or grounding_failed\n    states may be manually archived. Returns 400 for non-archivable states.\n    \"\"\"\n    protocol = db.get(Protocol, protocol_id)\n    if not protocol:\n        raise HTTPException(\n            status_code=404,\n            detail=f\"Protocol {protocol_id} not found\",\n        )\n\n    archivable_states = {\"dead_letter\", \"extraction_failed\", \"grounding_failed\"}\n    if protocol.status not in archivable_states:\n        raise HTTPException(\n            status_code=400,\n            detail=(\n                f\"Protocol cannot be archived from state '{protocol.status}'. \"\n                \"Archivable states: \" + \", \".join(sorted(archivable_states))\n            ),\n        )\n\n    protocol.status = \"archived\"\n    db.add(protocol)\n    db.commit()\n    db.refresh(protocol)\n\n    return _protocol_to_response(protocol)\n\n\n@router.post(\"/{protocol_id}/re-extract\", response_model=ReExtractResponse)\ndef re_extract_protocol(\n    protocol_id: str,\n    db: Session = Depends(get_db),\n) -> ReExtractResponse:\n    \"\"\"Trigger re-extraction on an existing protocol without re-uploading the PDF.\n\n    Archives all existing non-archived batches for this protocol (preserves them\n    in the database but hides them from the dashboard and review page). Collects\n    reviewed criteria from the archived batches to enable review inheritance.\n    Triggers the extraction pipeline via outbox event (same flow as initial upload).\n\n    The extraction pipeline will create a new batch. Review decisions from archived\n    criteria are auto-inherited for criteria with >90% fuzzy text match (same type).\n\n    Returns 404 if protocol not found.\n    Returns 409 if protocol is in a processing state (extracting, grounding) to\n    prevent race conditions — only terminal states are re-extractable.\n    \"\"\"\n    protocol = db.get(Protocol, protocol_id)\n    if not protocol:\n        raise HTTPException(\n            status_code=404,\n            detail=f\"Protocol {protocol_id} not found\",\n        )\n\n    # Only allow re-extraction from terminal states — prevent race conditions\n    # with an in-progress pipeline run (Pitfall 5 from RESEARCH.md)\n    terminal_states = {\n        \"pending_review\",\n        \"complete\",\n        \"extraction_failed\",\n        \"grounding_failed\",\n        \"dead_letter\",\n    }\n    if protocol.status not in terminal_states:\n        raise HTTPException(\n            status_code=409,\n            detail=(\n                f\"Protocol is not in a terminal state (current: {protocol.status}). \"\n                \"Re-extraction is only allowed for: \"\n                + \", \".join(sorted(terminal_states))\n            ),\n        )\n\n    # Find all non-archived batches for this protocol\n    existing_batches = db.exec(\n        select(CriteriaBatch).where(\n            CriteriaBatch.protocol_id == protocol_id,\n            CriteriaBatch.is_archived == False,  # noqa: E712\n        )\n    ).all()\n\n    # Collect reviewed criteria from batches being archived (for inheritance)\n    archived_reviewed_criteria: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\n    for batch in existing_batches:\n        criteria = db.exec(\n            select(Criteria).where(\n                Criteria.batch_id == batch.id,\n                col(Criteria.review_status).isnot(None),\n            )\n        ).all()\n        for criterion in criteria:\n            archived_reviewed_criteria.append(\n                {\n                    \"id\": criterion.id,\n                    \"text\": criterion.text,\n                    \"criteria_type\": criterion.criteria_type,\n                    \"review_status\": criterion.review_status,\n                    \"reviewed_by\": None,  # Review reviewer_id tracked in Review table\n                }\n            )\n\n    # Archive all existing non-archived batches\n    archived_count = len(existing_batches)\n    for batch in existing_batches:\n        batch.is_archived = True\n        db.add(batch)\n\n    logger.info(\n        \"Archiving %d batch(es) for protocol %s before re-extraction \"\n        \"(collected %d reviewed criteria for inheritance)\",\n        archived_count,\n        protocol_id,\n        len(archived_reviewed_criteria),\n    )\n\n    # Update protocol status to extracting and trigger extraction pipeline\n    protocol.status = \"extracting\"\n    protocol.error_reason = None\n\n    # Trigger extraction via outbox event — reuses existing pipeline\n    # (same as initial upload flow). Include archived reviewed criteria in\n    # payload so the persist node can apply review inheritance after batch creation.\n    persist_with_outbox(\n        session=db,\n        entity=protocol,\n        event_type=DomainEventKind.PROTOCOL_UPLOADED,\n        aggregate_type=\"protocol\",\n        aggregate_id=protocol.id,\n        payload={\n            \"protocol_id\": protocol.id,\n            \"title\": protocol.title,\n            \"file_uri\": protocol.file_uri,\n            \"archived_reviewed_criteria\": archived_reviewed_criteria,\n        },\n    )\n\n    db.commit()\n\n    return ReExtractResponse(\n        status=\"extracting\",\n        protocol_id=protocol_id,\n        archived_batches=archived_count,\n    )\n\n\n@router.get(\"\", response_model=ProtocolListResponse)\ndef list_protocols(\n    page: int = Query(default=1, ge=1),\n    page_size: int = Query(default=20, ge=1, le=100),\n    status: str | None = Query(default=None),\n    deduplicate: bool = Query(default=False),\n    db: Session = Depends(get_db),\n) -> ProtocolListResponse:\n    \"\"\"List protocols with pagination and optional status filter.\n\n    Returns a paginated list sorted by creation date (newest first).\n    Includes total count, page number, page size, and total pages.\n    Excludes archived protocols from default view unless explicitly filtered.\n\n    When deduplicate=True, returns one protocol per unique title (the latest\n    version) with a version_count field indicating how many versions exist.\n    Archived protocols are excluded from deduplication queries.\n    \"\"\"\n    if deduplicate:\n        return _list_protocols_deduplicated(page, page_size, status, db)\n\n    # Build count query\n    count_stmt = select(func.count()).select_from(Protocol)\n    if status:\n        count_stmt = count_stmt.where(Protocol.status == status)\n    else:\n        # Exclude archived protocols from default view\n        count_stmt = count_stmt.where(Protocol.status != \"archived\")\n    total = db.exec(count_stmt).one()\n\n    # Build data query\n    data_stmt = select(Protocol)\n    if status:\n        data_stmt = data_stmt.where(Protocol.status == status)\n    else:\n        # Exclude archived protocols from default view\n        data_stmt = data_stmt.where(Protocol.status != \"archived\")\n    data_stmt = (\n        data_stmt.offset((page - 1) * page_size)\n        .limit(page_size)\n        .order_by(Protocol.created_at.desc())  # type: ignore[attr-defined]\n    )\n\n    protocols = db.exec(data_stmt).all()\n    pages = ceil(total / page_size) if total > 0 else 1\n\n    return ProtocolListResponse(\n        items=[_protocol_to_response(p) for p in protocols],\n        total=total,\n        page=page,\n        page_size=page_size,\n        pages=pages,\n    )\n\n\ndef _list_protocols_deduplicated(\n    page: int,\n    page_size: int,\n    status: str | None,\n    db: Session,\n) -> ProtocolListResponse:\n    \"\"\"Return one protocol per unique title (latest version) with version_count.\n\n    Excludes archived protocols. When status filter is provided, only titles\n    whose latest version matches that status are returned.\n    \"\"\"\n    # Subquery: latest created_at per title (excluding archived)\n    latest_subq = (\n        select(Protocol.title, func.max(Protocol.created_at).label(\"max_created\"))\n        .where(Protocol.status != \"archived\")\n        .group_by(Protocol.title)\n        .subquery()\n    )\n\n    # Count subquery: number of (non-archived) versions per title\n    count_subq = (\n        select(Protocol.title, func.count(col(Protocol.id)).label(\"version_count\"))\n        .where(Protocol.status != \"archived\")\n        .group_by(Protocol.title)\n        .subquery()\n    )\n\n    # Join Protocol to latest_subq to get the canonical (newest) row per title\n    dedup_stmt = select(Protocol).join(\n        latest_subq,\n        (col(Protocol.title) == latest_subq.c.title)\n        & (col(Protocol.created_at) == latest_subq.c.max_created),\n    )\n\n    if status:\n        dedup_stmt = dedup_stmt.where(Protocol.status == status)\n\n    # Count total unique titles (for pagination)\n    count_stmt = select(func.count()).select_from(dedup_stmt.subquery())\n    total = db.exec(count_stmt).one()\n\n    # Apply pagination and ordering\n    dedup_stmt = (\n        dedup_stmt.order_by(Protocol.created_at.desc())  # type: ignore[attr-defined]\n        .offset((page - 1) * page_size)\n        .limit(page_size)\n    )\n\n    protocols = db.exec(dedup_stmt).all()\n    pages = ceil(total / page_size) if total > 0 else 1\n\n    # Build version_count lookup from count subquery\n    version_counts: dict[str, int] = {\n        row[0]: row[1]\n        for row in db.exec(select(count_subq.c.title, count_subq.c.version_count)).all()\n    }\n\n    return ProtocolListResponse(\n        items=[\n            _protocol_to_response(p, version_count=version_counts.get(p.title, 1))\n            for p in protocols\n        ],\n        total=total,\n        page=page,\n        page_size=page_size,\n        pages=pages,\n    )\n\n\n@router.get(\"/{protocol_id}\", response_model=ProtocolResponse)\ndef get_protocol(\n    protocol_id: str,\n    db: Session = Depends(get_db),\n) -> ProtocolResponse:\n    \"\"\"Get protocol detail including quality score and metadata.\n\n    Performs lazy archival: if a protocol is in dead_letter status and\n    hasn't been updated in 7+ days, it is automatically transitioned to archived.\n    \"\"\"\n    protocol = db.get(Protocol, protocol_id)\n    if not protocol:\n        raise HTTPException(\n            status_code=404,\n            detail=f\"Protocol {protocol_id} not found\",\n        )\n\n    # Check for dead-letter archival\n    _check_dead_letter_archival(protocol, db)\n\n    return _protocol_to_response(protocol)\n\n\n@router.get(\n    \"/{protocol_id}/batches\",\n    response_model=list[BatchSummaryResponse],\n)\ndef list_protocol_batches(\n    protocol_id: str,\n    db: Session = Depends(get_db),\n) -> list[BatchSummaryResponse]:\n    \"\"\"List ALL batches for a protocol including archived ones.\n\n    Returns batches in chronological order (oldest first) for the batch\n    timeline view. Unlike GET /reviews/batches which excludes archived\n    batches for the review workflow, this endpoint includes them so that\n    reviewers can see the full re-extraction history.\n\n    This is intentionally separate from the existing batch listing endpoint\n    to preserve the review workflow's correct exclusion of archived batches\n    (per research anti-pattern guidance).\n\n    Returns 404 if protocol doesn't exist.\n    \"\"\"\n    # Verify protocol exists\n    protocol = db.get(Protocol, protocol_id)\n    if not protocol:\n        raise HTTPException(\n            status_code=404,\n            detail=f\"Protocol {protocol_id} not found\",\n        )\n\n    # Load ALL batches for this protocol (no is_archived filter)\n    # ordered chronologically ascending for timeline display\n    batches = db.exec(\n        select(CriteriaBatch)\n        .where(CriteriaBatch.protocol_id == protocol_id)\n        .order_by(col(CriteriaBatch.created_at).asc())\n    ).all()\n\n    if not batches:\n        return []\n\n    # Batch-load criteria counts to avoid N+1 queries\n    batch_ids = [b.id for b in batches]\n\n    # Total criteria count per batch\n    total_counts_stmt = (\n        select(Criteria.batch_id, func.count(col(Criteria.id)).label(\"cnt\"))\n        .where(col(Criteria.batch_id).in_(batch_ids))\n        .group_by(Criteria.batch_id)\n    )\n    total_counts: dict[str, int] = {\n        row[0]: row[1] for row in db.exec(total_counts_stmt).all()\n    }\n\n    # Reviewed criteria count per batch (review_status IS NOT NULL)\n    reviewed_counts_stmt = (\n        select(Criteria.batch_id, func.count(col(Criteria.id)).label(\"cnt\"))\n        .where(\n            col(Criteria.batch_id).in_(batch_ids),\n            col(Criteria.review_status).isnot(None),\n        )\n        .group_by(Criteria.batch_id)\n    )\n    reviewed_counts: dict[str, int] = {\n        row[0]: row[1] for row in db.exec(reviewed_counts_stmt).all()\n    }\n\n    return [\n        BatchSummaryResponse(\n            id=batch.id,\n            protocol_id=batch.protocol_id,\n            status=batch.status,\n            is_archived=batch.is_archived,\n            criteria_count=total_counts.get(batch.id, 0),\n            reviewed_count=reviewed_counts.get(batch.id, 0),\n            extraction_model=batch.extraction_model,\n            created_at=batch.created_at,\n        )\n        for batch in batches\n    ]\n\n\n# --- Helpers ---\n\n\ndef _protocol_to_response(\n    protocol: Protocol, version_count: int = 1\n) -> ProtocolResponse:\n    \"\"\"Convert a Protocol model to ProtocolResponse.\"\"\"\n    return ProtocolResponse(\n        id=protocol.id,\n        title=protocol.title,\n        file_uri=protocol.file_uri,\n        status=protocol.status,\n        page_count=protocol.page_count,\n        quality_score=protocol.quality_score,\n        metadata_=protocol.metadata_,\n        created_at=protocol.created_at,\n        updated_at=protocol.updated_at,\n        error_reason=protocol.error_reason,\n        version_count=version_count,\n    )\n\n\ndef _check_dead_letter_archival(protocol: Protocol, session: Session) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Check if a dead-letter protocol should be archived.\n\n    Archives protocols that have been in dead_letter status for more than 7 days\n    since their last update. This is a lazy archival strategy triggered on access.\n    \"\"\"\n    if protocol.status != \"dead_letter\":\n        return\n\n    # Check if updated_at is more than 7 days ago\n    cutoff = datetime.now(timezone.utc) - timedelta(days=7)\n    if protocol.updated_at < cutoff:\n        logger.info(\n            \"Archiving dead-letter protocol %s (last updated: %s)\",\n            protocol.id,\n            protocol.updated_at,\n        )\n        protocol.status = \"archived\"\n        session.add(protocol)\n        session.commit()\n\n\ndef _apply_review_inheritance(\n    db: Session,\n    protocol_id: str,\n    archived_criteria: list[dict[str, Any]],\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Auto-inherit review decisions from archived criteria to new batch criteria.\n\n    Finds the newest non-archived batch for the protocol, then uses fuzzy\n    matching to identify matching criteria from the archived set. For each\n    match above the 90% threshold (same criteria_type), copies the review\n    decision to the new criterion and creates a Review record.\n\n    Called from the extraction pipeline after new batch/criteria are persisted\n    (e.g., from persist_node in extraction-service when archived_reviewed_criteria\n    is present in the outbox payload).\n\n    Args:\n        db: Database session.\n        protocol_id: Protocol to apply inheritance for.\n        archived_criteria: List of criterion dicts from archived batches.\n            Must have: id, text, criteria_type, review_status.\n    \"\"\"\n    if not archived_criteria:\n        return\n\n    # Find the newest non-archived batch for this protocol\n    newest_batch = db.exec(\n        select(CriteriaBatch)\n        .where(\n            CriteriaBatch.protocol_id == protocol_id,\n            CriteriaBatch.is_archived == False,  # noqa: E712\n        )\n        .order_by(col(CriteriaBatch.created_at).desc())\n        .limit(1)\n    ).first()\n\n    if not newest_batch:\n        logger.warning(\n            \"No non-archived batch found for protocol %s — skipping inheritance\",\n            protocol_id,\n        )\n        return\n\n    # Load new criteria from the newest batch\n    new_criteria_list = db.exec(\n        select(Criteria).where(Criteria.batch_id == newest_batch.id)\n    ).all()\n\n    if not new_criteria_list:\n        return\n\n    # Convert new criteria to dicts for fuzzy matching\n    new_criteria_dicts = [\n        {\n            \"id\": c.id,\n            \"text\": c.text,\n            \"criteria_type\": c.criteria_type,\n        }\n        for c in new_criteria_list\n    ]\n\n    # Run fuzzy matching to find inheritance candidates\n    matches = inherit_reviews_for_batch(\n        new_criteria=new_criteria_dicts,\n        archived_criteria=archived_criteria,\n    )\n\n    if not matches:\n        logger.info(\n            \"No review decisions inherited for protocol %s (0 matches found)\",\n            protocol_id,\n        )\n        return\n\n    # Build lookup for fast criterion access\n    criteria_by_id = {c.id: c for c in new_criteria_list}\n\n    # Apply inheritance: update criterion status and create Review records\n    inherited = 0\n    for match in matches:\n        criterion = criteria_by_id.get(match[\"new_criterion_id\"])\n        if not criterion:\n            continue\n\n        criterion.review_status = match[\"review_status\"]\n        db.add(criterion)\n\n        # Create Review record documenting the auto-inheritance\n        review = Review(\n            reviewer_id=\"system:re-extraction-inheritance\",\n            target_type=\"criteria\",\n            target_id=criterion.id,\n            action=match[\"review_status\"],  # e.g., \"approved\", \"rejected\", \"modified\"\n            before_value={\"review_status\": None},\n            after_value={\n                \"review_status\": match[\"review_status\"],\n                \"inherited_from\": match[\"old_criterion_id\"],\n                \"match_score\": match[\"match_score\"],\n            },\n            comment=(\n                f\"Auto-inherited from archived criterion {match['old_criterion_id']} \"\n                f\"(fuzzy match score: {match['match_score']:.1f}%)\"\n            ),\n        )\n        db.add(review)\n        inherited += 1\n\n    db.commit()\n\n    logger.info(\n        \"Inherited %d/%d review decisions for protocol %s\",\n        inherited,\n        len(new_criteria_list),\n        protocol_id,\n    )\n"
}

     1→"""Outbox processor for publishing pending domain events.
     2→
     3→Implements the transactional outbox pattern with at-least-once delivery.
     4→The OutboxProcessor polls the outbox table for pending events and dispatches
     5→them to registered handlers. The persist_with_outbox helper enables atomic
     6→entity + event writes in a single database transaction.
     7→"""
     8→
     9→from __future__ import annotations
    10→
    11→import asyncio
    12→import logging
    13→from datetime import datetime, timezone
    14→from typing import Any, Callable
    15→from uuid import uuid4
    16→
    17→from shared.models import OutboxEvent
    18→from sqlalchemy import Engine
    19→from sqlmodel import Session, col, select
    20→
    21→from events_py.models import DomainEventKind
    22→
    23→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    24→
    25→MAX_RETRIES = 3
    26→
    27→
    28→class OutboxProcessor:
    29→    """Processes pending outbox events with at-least-once delivery.
    30→
    31→    Usage:
    32→        processor = OutboxProcessor(engine, handlers={
    33→            "protocol_uploaded": [handle_protocol_uploaded],
    34→        })
    35→        await processor.start()  # Runs polling loop
    36→        await processor.stop()   # Graceful shutdown
    37→    """
    38→
    39→    def __init__(
    40→        self,
    41→        engine: Engine,
    42→        handlers: dict[str, list[Callable[..., Any]]] | None = None,
    43→        poll_interval: float = 1.0,
    44→        batch_size: int = 100,
    45→    ) -> None:
    46→        """Initialize the outbox processor.
    47→
    48→        Args:
    49→            engine: SQLAlchemy database engine.
    50→            handlers: Maps event_type string to handler callbacks.
    51→            poll_interval: Seconds between polls.
    52→            batch_size: Max events per poll cycle.
    53→        """
    54→        self.engine = engine
    55→        self.handlers: dict[str, list[Callable[..., Any]]] = handlers or {}
    56→        self.poll_interval = poll_interval
    57→        self.batch_size = batch_size
    58→        self._shutdown_event = asyncio.Event()
    59→
    60→    def poll_and_process(self) -> int:
    61→        """Poll for pending events and process them.
    62→
    63→        Returns:
    64→            Number of events processed in this poll cycle.
    65→        """
    66→        processed = 0
    67→        with Session(self.engine) as session:
    68→            statement = (
    69→                select(OutboxEvent)
    70→                .where(col(OutboxEvent.status).in_(["pending", "failed"]))
    71→                .order_by(OutboxEvent.created_at.asc())  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
    72→                .limit(self.batch_size)
    73→            )
    74→
    75→            # Use FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED on PostgreSQL
    76→            # for concurrent processor safety
    77→            db_url = str(self.engine.url)
    78→            if "postgresql" in db_url:
    79→                statement = statement.with_for_update(skip_locked=True)
    80→
    81→            events = session.exec(statement).all()
    82→
    83→            for event in events:
    84→                event_handlers = self.handlers.get(event.event_type, [])
    85→
    86→                if not event_handlers:
    87→                    logger.debug(
    88→                        "No handlers for event type: %s",
    89→                        event.event_type,
    90→                    )
    91→
    92→                try:
    93→                    for handler in event_handlers:
    94→                        handler(event.payload)
    95→
    96→                    event.status = "published"
    97→                    event.published_at = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
    98→                    session.add(event)
    99→                    processed += 1
   100→
   101→                    logger.info(
   102→                        "Published event %s (type=%s)",
   103→                        event.id,
   104→                        event.event_type,
   105→                    )
   106→                except Exception:
   107→                    event.retry_count += 1
   108→                    if event.retry_count >= MAX_RETRIES:
   109→                        event.status = "dead_letter"
   110→                        logger.warning(
   111→                            "Event %s exhausted retries (%d), marking as dead_letter",
   112→                            event.id,
   113→                            event.retry_count,
   114→                        )
   115→                        # Update protocol status to dead_letter if protocol event
   116→                        if event.aggregate_type == "protocol":
   117→                            from shared.models import Protocol
   118→
   119→                            protocol = session.get(Protocol, event.aggregate_id)
   120→                            if protocol:
   121→                                protocol.status = "dead_letter"
   122→                                protocol.error_reason = "Maximum retries exceeded"
   123→                                protocol.metadata_ = {
   124→                                    **protocol.metadata_,
   125→                                    "error": {
   126→                                        "category": "pipeline_failed",
   127→                                        "reason": "Maximum retries exceeded",
   128→                                        "retry_count": event.retry_count,
   129→                                    },
   130→                                }
   131→                                session.add(protocol)
   132→                    else:
   133→                        event.status = "failed"
   134→
   135→                    session.add(event)
   136→                    logger.exception(
   137→                        "Failed to process event %s (type=%s, retry_count=%d)",
   138→                        event.id,
   139→                        event.event_type,
   140→                        event.retry_count,
   141→                    )
   142→
   143→            session.commit()
   144→
   145→        return processed
   146→
   147→    async def start(self) -> None:
   148→        """Run the polling loop until stop() is called."""
   149→        logger.info(
   150→            "Outbox processor started (interval=%.1fs, batch_size=%d)",
   151→            self.poll_interval,
   152→            self.batch_size,
   153→        )
   154→        while not self._shutdown_event.is_set():
   155→            try:
   156→                count = await asyncio.get_event_loop().run_in_executor(
   157→                    None, self.poll_and_process
   158→                )
   159→                if count > 0:
   160→                    logger.info("Processed %d outbox events", count)
   161→            except Exception:
   162→                logger.exception("Error in outbox processor poll cycle")
   163→            try:
   164→                await asyncio.wait_for(
   165→                    self._shutdown_event.wait(),
   166→                    timeout=self.poll_interval,
   167→                )
   168→            except asyncio.TimeoutError:
   169→                pass
   170→        logger.info("Outbox processor stopped")
   171→
   172→    async def stop(self) -> None:
   173→        """Signal the processor to stop after current poll."""
   174→        logger.info("Outbox processor shutdown requested")
   175→        self._shutdown_event.set()
   176→
   177→
   178→def persist_with_outbox(
   179→    session: Session,
   180→    entity: Any,
   181→    event_type: DomainEventKind,
   182→    aggregate_type: str,
   183→    aggregate_id: str,
   184→    payload: dict[str, Any],
   185→    idempotency_key: str | None = None,
   186→) -> OutboxEvent:
   187→    """Persist an entity and its outbox event in the same transaction.
   188→
   189→    The caller is responsible for committing the session.
   190→
   191→    Args:
   192→        session: Active SQLModel session (not yet committed).
   193→        entity: The SQLModel entity to persist.
   194→        event_type: Domain event kind.
   195→        aggregate_type: Type of aggregate (e.g. "protocol").
   196→        aggregate_id: ID of the affected aggregate.
   197→        payload: Event payload dict.
   198→        idempotency_key: Dedup key; auto-generated if omitted.
   199→
   200→    Returns:
   201→        The created OutboxEvent (uncommitted).
   202→    """
   203→    ikey = idempotency_key or (f"{event_type.value}:{aggregate_id}:{uuid4()}")
   204→
   205→    outbox_event = OutboxEvent(
   206→        event_type=event_type.value,
   207→        aggregate_type=aggregate_type,
   208→        aggregate_id=aggregate_id,
   209→        payload=payload,
   210→        idempotency_key=ikey,
   211→        status="pending",
   212→    )
   213→
   214→    session.add(entity)
   215→    session.add(outbox_event)
   216→
   217→    return outbox_event
   218→
```

    85→    return workflow.compile(checkpointer=checkpointer)
    86→
    87→
    88→# Singleton instances for reuse across outbox handler invocations.
    89→# Per Pitfall 1 from research: checkpointer is singleton, created once,
    90→# reused across all invocations. Do NOT create per-invocation.
    91→_graph = None
    92→_checkpointer = None
    93→# Context manager for PostgresSaver; kept entered for app lifetime.
    94→_checkpointer_cm: Any = None
    95→
    96→
    97→async def _get_checkpointer_async() -> Any:
    98→    """Get or create the AsyncPostgresSaver checkpointer singleton.
    99→
   100→    Creates an AsyncPostgresSaver using DATABASE_URL from the environment.
   101→    Uses the async context manager and calls asetup() to ensure checkpoint
   102→    tables exist. The context is kept open for the process lifetime.
   103→
   104→    Returns:
   105→        AsyncPostgresSaver instance configured with the application database.
   106→
   107→    Raises:
   108→        KeyError: If DATABASE_URL environment variable is not set.
   109→    """
   110→    global _checkpointer, _checkpointer_cm  # noqa: PLW0603
   111→    if _checkpointer is None:
   112→        from langgraph.checkpoint.postgres.aio import AsyncPostgresSaver
   113→
   114→        db_url = os.environ["DATABASE_URL"]
   115→        _checkpointer_cm = AsyncPostgresSaver.from_conn_string(db_url)
   116→        _checkpointer = await _checkpointer_cm.__aenter__()
   117→        await _checkpointer.asetup()  # Create checkpoint tables if they don't exist
   118→    return _checkpointer
   119→
   120→
   121→async def get_graph() -> Any:
   122→    """Get or create the compiled graph singleton.
   123→
   124→    Compiles the graph with an AsyncPostgresSaver checkpointer if DATABASE_URL
   125→    is available. Falls back to no checkpointer if DATABASE_URL is not set
   126→    (e.g. in unit tests).
   127→
   128→    Returns:
   129→        Compiled StateGraph instance.
   130→    """
   131→    global _graph  # noqa: PLW0603
   132→    if _graph is None:
   133→        try:
   134→            checkpointer = await _get_checkpointer_async()
   135→        except (KeyError, Exception):
   136→            # DATABASE_URL not set (e.g. unit tests) — compile without checkpointer
   137→            checkpointer = None
   138→        _graph = create_graph(checkpointer=checkpointer)
   139→    return _graph
   140→

     1→"""Unified outbox event handler for ProtocolUploaded events.
     2→
     3→Replaces the two-service trigger pattern (extraction_service + grounding_service)
     4→with a single consolidated handler that invokes the full 5-node pipeline:
     5→ingest -> extract -> parse -> ground -> persist
     6→
     7→Per user decision (v2.0): "Remove criteria_extracted outbox, retain protocol_uploaded"
     8→
     9→The handler is synchronous (called by OutboxProcessor.poll_and_process) and
    10→bridges to the async graph via asyncio.run(). This works without event loop
    11→conflicts because the outbox processor runs handlers in a thread executor
    12→via run_in_executor, so there is no existing event loop in the current thread.
    13→
    14→Checkpointing: Each invocation generates a unique thread_id (protocol_id:uuid4)
    15→and stores it in protocol.metadata_ so that retry_from_checkpoint can look it up.
    16→This prevents checkpoint collision when re-extracting the same protocol.
    17→"""
    18→
    19→from __future__ import annotations
    20→
    21→import asyncio
    22→import logging
    23→import os
    24→from typing import Any
    25→from uuid import uuid4
    26→
    27→from api_service.storage import engine
    28→from shared.models import Protocol
    29→from sqlmodel import Session
    30→
    31→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    32→
    33→
    34→def _cleanup_orphan_traces() -> None:
    35→    """Close stale IN_PROGRESS MLflow traces from previous crashed runs.
    36→
    37→    Runs once at module import (service startup). Idempotent -- safe to
    38→    call multiple times. Only closes traces older than 1 hour to avoid
    39→    closing currently-running pipelines.
    40→    """
    41→    import time as _time
    42→
    43→    tracking_uri = os.getenv("MLFLOW_TRACKING_URI")
    44→    if not tracking_uri:
    45→        return
    46→    try:
    47→        import mlflow
    48→
    49→        client = mlflow.MlflowClient()
    50→        cutoff_ms = int((_time.time() - 3600) * 1000)  # 1 hour ago
    51→
    52→        # MLflow 3.x: search for traces with IN_PROGRESS status
    53→        try:
    54→            traces = client.search_traces(
    55→                experiment_ids=["0"],  # Default experiment
    56→                filter_string="status = 'IN_PROGRESS'",
    57→                max_results=50,
    58→            )
    59→        except (AttributeError, TypeError):
    60→            # search_traces may not exist or have different signature
    61→            logger.debug("MLflow search_traces API not available for orphan cleanup")
    62→            return
    63→
    64→        closed = 0
    65→        for trace in traces:
    66→            trace_ts = getattr(trace.info, "timestamp_ms", None) or 0
    67→            if trace_ts < cutoff_ms:
    68→                try:
    69→                    client.end_trace(
    70→                        request_id=trace.info.request_id,
    71→                        status="ERROR",
    72→                    )
    73→                    closed += 1
    74→                except Exception:
    75→                    pass  # Trace may have already been closed
    76→        if closed:
    77→            logger.info(
    78→                "Startup orphan cleanup: closed %d stale IN_PROGRESS trace(s)",
    79→                closed,
    80→            )
    81→    except Exception:
    82→        logger.debug("Orphan trace cleanup skipped (non-fatal)", exc_info=True)
    83→
    84→
    85→# Run once at startup to clean up orphaned traces from previous crashes
    86→_cleanup_orphan_traces()
    87→
    88→
    89→def _ensure_mlflow() -> bool:
    90→    """Ensure MLflow tracking is configured in the current thread.
    91→
    92→    Returns True if MLflow tracing is available and configured.
    93→    """
    94→    try:
    95→        import mlflow
    96→
    97→        tracking_uri = os.getenv("MLFLOW_TRACKING_URI")
    98→        if tracking_uri:
    99→            mlflow.set_tracking_uri(tracking_uri)
   100→            mlflow.set_experiment("protocol-processing")
   101→            return True
   102→    except ImportError:
   103→        pass
   104→    return False
   105→
   106→
   107→def _categorize_pipeline_error(e: Exception) -> str:
   108→    """Convert exception to human-readable pipeline error reason.
   109→
   110→    Combines extraction and grounding error categorization from both
   111→    the old extraction_service and grounding_service triggers.
   112→
   113→    Args:
   114→        e: The exception that occurred during pipeline execution.
   115→
   116→    Returns:
   117→        Human-readable error message for the user.
   118→    """
   119→    error_str = str(e).lower()
   120→
   121→    # PDF / extraction errors
   122→    if "pdf" in error_str or "pymupdf" in error_str:
   123→        return "PDF text quality too low or file corrupted"
   124→    if "gcs" in error_str or "storage" in error_str or "bucket" in error_str:
   125→        return "File storage service unavailable"
   126→
   127→    # Auth / credential errors
   128→    if "credential" in error_str or "auth" in error_str:
   129→        return "Service authentication failed"
   130→
   131→    # UMLS / grounding errors
   132→    if "mcp" in error_str or "subprocess" in error_str:
   133→        return "UMLS grounding service unavailable"
   134→    if "concept_linking" in error_str or "concept_search" in error_str:
   135→        return "UMLS terminology service unavailable"
   136→
   137→    # Generic transient errors
   138→    if "circuit" in error_str:
   139→        return "AI service temporarily unavailable"
   140→    if "timeout" in error_str or "timed out" in error_str:
   141→        return "Processing timed out"
   142→    if "parse" in error_str:
   143→        return "Protocol parsing failed"
   144→
   145→    return f"Pipeline failed: {type(e).__name__}"
   146→
   147→
   148→def _update_protocol_failed(
   149→    protocol_id: str,
   150→    reason: str,
   151→    error_category: str,
   152→    exception_type: str,
   153→) -> None:
   154→    """Update protocol status to extraction_failed with error metadata.
   155→
   156→    Args:
   157→        protocol_id: Protocol ID to update.
   158→        reason: Human-readable error reason.
   159→        error_category: Short category string for the error type.
   160→        exception_type: Python exception class name.
   161→    """
   162→    try:
   163→        with Session(engine) as session:
   164→            protocol = session.get(Protocol, protocol_id)
   165→            if protocol:
   166→                protocol.status = "extraction_failed"
   167→                protocol.error_reason = reason
   168→                protocol.metadata_ = {
   169→                    **protocol.metadata_,
   170→                    "error": {
   171→                        "category": error_category,
   172→                        "reason": reason,
   173→                        "exception_type": exception_type,
   174→                    },
   175→                }
   176→                session.add(protocol)
   177→                session.commit()
   178→    except Exception:
   179→        logger.exception(
   180→            "Failed to update protocol %s status to extraction_failed",
   181→            protocol_id,
   182→        )
   183→
   184→
   185→# KNOWN LIMITATION: MLflow ContextVar async boundary (Issue #8)
   186→#
   187→# MLflow's trace context uses Python ContextVars which do NOT propagate
   188→# across asyncio task boundaries created by asyncio.create_task() or
   189→# asyncio.gather(). This means:
   190→#   - The parent trace in trigger.py correctly wraps the full pipeline run
   191→#   - Child spans created inside LangGraph nodes are attached to the parent
   192→#     trace because LangGraph uses `await` (not create_task) for node calls
   193→#   - However, if future code uses asyncio.gather() for parallel node
   194→#     execution, child spans may detach from the parent trace
   195→#   - Workaround: Pass trace context explicitly via PipelineState if parallel
   196→#     node execution is added
   197→#
   198→# This is a known MLflow limitation, not a bug in our code.
   199→
   200→
   201→async def _run_pipeline(
   202→    graph: Any,
   203→    initial_state: dict[str, Any],
   204→    config: dict[str, Any],
   205→    payload: dict[str, Any],
   206→) -> dict[str, Any]:
   207→    """Run the pipeline graph with MLflow tracing inside the async context.
   208→
   209→    MLflow ContextVars must be created in the same async context as the
   210→    LangGraph invocation. asyncio.run() creates an isolated context, so
   211→    we initialize MLflow tracing HERE (inside asyncio.run) rather than
   212→    in the sync caller.
   213→    """
   214→    if _ensure_mlflow():
   215→        import mlflow
   216→
   217→        mlflow.langchain.autolog(run_tracer_inline=True)
   218→
   219→        with mlflow.start_span(
   220→            name="protocol_pipeline",
   221→            span_type="CHAIN",
   222→        ) as span:
   223→            # Capture trace_id immediately so finally block can force-close
   224→            # orphaned traces on process-kill or unexpected exception.
   225→            trace_id = mlflow.get_active_trace_id()
   226→            span.set_inputs(
   227→                {
   228→                    "protocol_id": payload.get("protocol_id", ""),
   229→                    "title": payload.get("title", ""),
   230→                    "file_uri": payload.get("file_uri", ""),
   231→                }
   232→            )
   233→            succeeded = False
   234→            try:
   235→                result = await graph.ainvoke(initial_state, config)
   236→                span.set_outputs(
   237→                    {
   238→                        "status": result.get("status"),
   239→                        "error": result.get("error"),
   240→                        "error_count": len(result.get("errors", [])),
   241→                    }
   242→                )
   243→                succeeded = True
   244→                return result
   245→            finally:
   246→                # Only force-close as ERROR on failure — let the context
   247→                # manager's __exit__ handle successful traces naturally.
   248→                if trace_id and not succeeded:
   249→                    logger.warning(
   250→                        "Force-closing MLflow trace %s as ERROR (pipeline failed)",
   251→                        trace_id,
   252→                    )
   253→                    try:
   254→                        mlflow.MlflowClient().end_trace(trace_id, status="ERROR")
   255→                    except Exception:
   256→                        logger.warning(
   257→                            "Could not force-close MLflow trace %s"
   258→                            " — already closed or unavailable",
   259→                            trace_id,
   260→                        )
   261→
   262→    return await graph.ainvoke(initial_state, config)
   263→
   264→
   265→def handle_protocol_uploaded(payload: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
   266→    """Handle a ProtocolUploaded event by running the full pipeline.
   267→
   268→    Constructs the initial PipelineState from the event payload and
   269→    invokes the consolidated 5-node LangGraph workflow via asyncio.run().
   270→
   271→    Uses protocol_id as the LangGraph thread_id so that retry_from_checkpoint
   272→    can locate the saved checkpoint by protocol_id alone.
   273→
   274→    Replaces both extraction_service.trigger.handle_protocol_uploaded and
   275→    grounding_service.trigger.handle_criteria_extracted from the v1.x
   276→    two-service architecture.
   277→
   278→    Args:
   279→        payload: Event payload dict containing protocol_id, file_uri, and title.
   280→
   281→    Raises:
   282→        Exception: Re-raised after logging to let the outbox processor
   283→            mark the event as failed for retry.
   284→    """
   285→    protocol_id = payload.get("protocol_id", "unknown")
   286→    logger.info(
   287→        "Handling ProtocolUploaded event for protocol %s (consolidated pipeline)",
   288→        protocol_id,
   289→    )
   290→
   291→    try:
   292→        # Generate unique thread_id per pipeline run to prevent checkpoint collision
   293→        # on re-extraction (same protocol_id would resume old completed checkpoint)
   294→        thread_id = f"{protocol_id}:{uuid4()}"
   295→
   296→        initial_state: dict[str, Any] = {
   297→            "protocol_id": payload["protocol_id"],
   298→            "file_uri": payload["file_uri"],
   299→            "title": payload["title"],
   300→            "batch_id": None,
   301→            "pdf_bytes": None,
   302→            "extraction_json": None,
   303→            "entities_json": None,
   304→            "grounded_entities_json": None,
   305→            "archived_reviewed_criteria": payload.get("archived_reviewed_criteria"),
   306→            "status": "processing",
   307→            "error": None,
   308→            "errors": [],
   309→        }
   310→
   311→        # Store thread_id in protocol metadata for retry_from_checkpoint lookup
   312→        try:
   313→            with Session(engine) as session:
   314→                protocol = session.get(Protocol, protocol_id)
   315→                if protocol:
   316→                    meta = protocol.metadata_ or {}
   317→                    protocol.metadata_ = {
   318→                        **meta,
   319→                        "pipeline_thread_id": thread_id,
   320→                    }
   321→                    session.add(protocol)
   322→                    session.commit()
   323→        except Exception:
   324→            logger.warning(
   325→                "Failed to store pipeline_thread_id for protocol %s",
   326→                protocol_id,
   327→            )
   328→
   329→        # Lazy import to avoid circular imports at module load time
   330→        from protocol_processor.graph import get_graph
   331→
   332→        graph = get_graph()
   333→
   334→        config = {"configurable": {"thread_id": thread_id}}
   335→
   336→        asyncio.run(_run_pipeline(graph, initial_state, config, payload))
   337→
   338→        logger.info(
   339→            "Protocol pipeline completed for protocol %s",
   340→            protocol_id,
   341→        )
   342→
   343→    except Exception as e:
   344→        logger.exception(
   345→            "Protocol pipeline failed for protocol %s",
   346→            protocol_id,
   347→        )
   348→        reason = _categorize_pipeline_error(e)
   349→        _update_protocol_failed(
   350→            protocol_id,
   351→            reason,
   352→            "pipeline_failed",
   353→            type(e).__name__,
   354→        )
   355→        raise
   356→
   357→
   358→async def retry_from_checkpoint(protocol_id: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
   359→    """Resume pipeline from last checkpoint for a failed protocol.
   360→
   361→    Passes None as the input state to graph.ainvoke, which tells LangGraph
   362→    to resume from the last saved checkpoint for the given thread_id instead
   363→    of starting from scratch. Reads thread_id from protocol.metadata_ where
   364→    it was stored during the original pipeline invocation.
   365→
   366→    Args:
   367→        protocol_id: Protocol ID — used to look up thread_id from metadata.
   368→
   369→    Returns:
   370→        Final pipeline state dict after resuming from checkpoint.
   371→    """
   372→    with Session(engine) as session:
   373→        protocol = session.get(Protocol, protocol_id)
   374→        if not protocol:
   375→            raise ValueError(f"Protocol {protocol_id} not found")
   376→        thread_id = (protocol.metadata_ or {}).get("pipeline_thread_id", protocol_id)
   377→
   378→    from protocol_processor.graph import get_graph
   379→
   380→    graph = get_graph()
   381→    config = {"configurable": {"thread_id": thread_id}}
   382→    # Pass None as input — LangGraph resumes from last checkpoint
   383→    result = await graph.ainvoke(None, config)
   384→    return result
   385→

     4→with a single consolidated handler that invokes the full 5-node pipeline:

     8→
     9→The handler is synchronous (called by OutboxProcessor.poll_and_process) and
    10→bridges to the async graph via asyncio.run(). This works without event loop
    11→conflicts because the outbox processor runs handlers in a thread executor
    12→via run_in_executor, so there is no existing event loop in the current thread.
    13→
    14→Checkpointing: Each invocation generates a unique thread_id (protocol_id:uuid4)
    15→and stores it in protocol.metadata_ so that retry_from_checkpoint can look it up.

    39→    try:

    49→    return False

    52→def _categorize_pipeline_error(e: Exception) -> str:
    53→    """Convert exception to human-readable pipeline error reason.
    54→
    55→    Combines extraction and grounding error categorization from both
    56→    the old extraction_service and grounding_service triggers.
    57→
    58→    Args:
    59→        e: The exception that occurred during pipeline execution.
    60→
    61→    Returns:

    63→    """
    64→    error_str = str(e).lower()
    65→
    66→    # PDF / extraction errors
    67→    if "pdf" in error_str or "pymupdf" in error_str:
    68→        return "PDF text quality too low or file corrupted"
    69→    if "gcs" in error_str or "storage" in error_str or "bucket" in error_str:
    70→        return "File storage service unavailable"
    71→
    72→    # Auth / credential errors
    73→    if "credential" in error_str or "auth" in error_str:
    74→        return "Service authentication failed"
    75→
    76→    # UMLS / grounding errors
    77→    if "mcp" in error_str or "subprocess" in error_str:
    78→        return "UMLS grounding service unavailable"
    79→    if "concept_linking" in error_str or "concept_search" in error_str:
    80→        return "UMLS terminology service unavailable"
    81→

    85→    if "timeout" in error_str or "timed out" in error_str:
    86→        return "Processing timed out"
    87→    if "parse" in error_str:
    88→        return "Protocol parsing failed"
    89→
    90→    return f"Pipeline failed: {type(e).__name__}"
